- **78:63** "Their young women had no wedding song" translates *betulotav lo hullalu*.
- **78:64** "Their priests fell by the sword" refers to the sons of Eli (1 Samuel 4:11).
- **78:65** "Then the Lord awoke as from sleep" is rendered as written. "Like a warrior shouting because of wine" translates *ke-gibbor mitronen mi-yayin*.
- **78:66** "He drove his adversaries back" translates *va-yak tsarav achor*, literally "he struck his foes backward", which may refer to the plague of 1 Samuel 5:6.
- **78:67** "The tent of Joseph" is the sanctuary of Shiloh in Ephraim.
- **78:68** "The tribe of Judah, Mount Zion" moves the centre of worship from Ephraim to Zion.
- **78:69** "Like the high heavens" translates *ke-ramim*, "like the heights".
- **78:72** "With an upright heart" translates *be-tom levavo*. "His skilful hand" translates *tevunot kappav*, "the skills of his hands".
- **79:1** "Your holy temple" translates *heikhal qodshekha*. See entry H-QADOSH. "Laid Jerusalem in ruins" translates *samu et-Yerushalaim le-iyyim*, "made Jerusalem heaps of ruins" (see Micah 3:12).
- **79:2** "Your faithful ones" translates *chasidekha*. See entry H-HESED. "The birds of the heavens" recalls Deuteronomy 28:26.
- **79:3** "There was no one to bury them" is the disgrace of the unburied dead.
- **79:6** The verses 6 and 7 are nearly the same as Jeremiah 10:25. The prayer against the nations is rendered as written.
- **79:8** "The iniquities of our ancestors" translates *avonot rishonim*, which may mean "former iniquities" or "the iniquities of the first ones".
- **79:9** "Atone for our sins" translates *kapper al-chatto'tenu*. See entry H-KAPPER.
- **79:10** "Where is their God?" is the mockery of the nations (see 42:3 and Joel 2:17). "Let the avenging of the outpoured blood of your servants be known among the nations" is a prayer for justice, rendered as written.
- **79:11** "Those doomed to die" translates *benei temutah*, "sons of death".
- **79:12** "Return sevenfold into the lap of our neighbours" is rendered as written, with no softening. "The lap" translates *cheq*, the fold of the garment.
- **80** "According to Lilies. A testimony" translates *el-shoshannim edut*, which names a tune (see Psalm 60:1).
- **80:1** "Shepherd of Israel" is a title of God (see Genesis 48:15 and 49:24). "Enthroned on the cherubim" refers to the ark of the covenant (see 1 Samuel 4:4).
- **80:2** "Ephraim and Benjamin and Manasseh" are the three tribes that marched behind the ark in the wilderness (see Numbers 2:17-24).
- **80:3** "Restore us" translates *hashivenu*, "turn us back" (see entry H-SHUV). The refrain is repeated in verses 7 and 19, with a growing name of God ("God", "God of hosts", "LORD God of hosts").
- **80:4** "How long will you be angry at the prayers of your people?" translates *ad-matai ashanta bi-tefillat ammekha*, in which the verb *ashan* means "smoke" (see 74:1).
- **80:5** "In full measure" translates *shalish*, literally "a third", a measure.
- **80:6** "An object of contention" translates *madon*.
- **80:8** "A vine out of Egypt" is a figure of Israel (see Isaiah 5:1-7 and Hosea 10:1).
- **80:11** "The River" is the Euphrates.
- **80:13** "The boar from the forest" translates *chazir mi-ya'ar*. The letter *ayin* in *ya'ar* is written above the line in the Hebrew text, as a scribal mark.
- **80:15** "The stock" translates *kannah*, a rare word that may mean "stock", "root" or "what your right hand planted". "The son whom you made strong for yourself" may refer to Israel or to the king.
- **80:17** "The man at your right hand, the son of man whom you made strong for yourself" may refer to the king or to the people. The Hebrew does not say which.
- **81** "According to the Gittith" is explained at Psalm 8.
- **81:3** "At the full moon" translates *ba-keseh*, a rare word. "Our feast day" is *chag*.
- **81:4** "A statute" is *choq* and "a rule" is *mishpat*.
- **81:5** "When he went out over the land of Egypt" may also be read "against the land of Egypt". "I hear a language I did not know" is the voice of the poet, who now hears God speak.
- **81:6** "The basket" translates *dud*, the basket for carrying bricks or clay.
- **81:7** "The secret place of thunder" translates *seter ra'am*. "Meribah" refers to Exodus 17:7 and Numbers 20:13.
- **81:9** "A strange god" translates *el zar*. The verses 9 and 10 recall the first commandment (Exodus 20:2-3).
- **81:12** "I gave them over to their stubborn hearts" is rendered as written (see Romans 1:24).
- **81:15** "Would cringe before him" translates *yekhachashu lo*. "Their fate would last for ever" translates *ve-hi ittam le-olam*, "and their time would be for ever".
- **81:16** "But he would feed you" has *ya'akhilehu*, with "he", and "I would satisfy you" has *asbi'ekha*, with "I". The speaker changes within the verse. "The finest of the wheat" translates *chelev chittah*, "the fat of the wheat".
- **82:1** "God has taken his place in the divine council" translates *Elohim nitsav ba-adat El*. "In the midst of the gods" translates *be-qerev elohim yishpot*. The word *elohim* may mean "gods", that is heavenly beings who rule the nations (see Deuteronomy 32:8-9 and Job 1:6), or human rulers and judges, since the word is used of judges in Exodus 21:6 and 22:8-9. The psalm is quoted in John 10:34-35.
- **82:2** "Show partiality to the wicked" translates *u-fenei resha'im tissa'u*, literally "lift the faces of the wicked".
- **82:3** "The weak and the fatherless" and "the afflicted and the destitute" are the people for whom the gods are to care.
- **82:5** "They have neither knowledge nor understanding" is a statement about the gods, or about the judges. "All the foundations of the earth are shaken" says that injustice shakes the order of the world.
- **82:6** "I said, 'You are gods, sons of the Most High, all of you'" translates *ani amarti elohim attem u-venei Elyon kullekhem*. It is God's own earlier word. It is quoted in John 10:34-35.
- **82:7** "Nevertheless, like human beings you will die, and fall like any prince" translates *akhen ke-adam temutun*. It is rendered as written. The verse may say that the gods lose their immortality or that the human rulers will die like other people. The Hebrew does not decide which.
- **82:8** "You will inherit all the nations" recalls Deuteronomy 32:8-9, in which the LORD's own portion was Israel.
- **83:2** "Have raised their heads" is a figure of open revolt.
- **83:3** "Those you treasure" translates *tsefunekha*, "your hidden ones", those whom God keeps safe.
- **83:4** "Let us wipe them out as a nation" is the plan of the enemies.
- **83:5** "Against you they make a covenant" uses *berit*. See entry H-BERIT.
- **83:6** "The Hagrites" are a people east of Gilead (see 1 Chronicles 5:10, 19).
- **83:7** "Gebal" may be the Phoenician city of Byblos or a place in Edom.
- **83:8** "The children of Lot" are Moab and Ammon (see Genesis 19:36-38). The league of ten nations has no exact match in the history, and the date of the psalm is not certain.
- **83:9** "Do to them as you did to Midian" refers to Judges 7, and "Sisera and Jabin at the river Kishon" to Judges 4 and 5.
- **83:10** "En-dor" is not named in the books of Judges and Samuel in this connection. "Dung for the ground" translates *domen la-adamah*, and the line is rendered as written.
- **83:11** "Oreb and Zeeb" and "Zebah and Zalmunna" are the princes and kings of Midian (see Judges 7:25 and 8:21).
- **83:12** "The pastures of God" translates *ne'ot Elohim*, the land of Israel.
- **83:13** "Whirling dust" translates *galgal*, which may also mean "a rolling thing" or "wheel".
- **83:15** "Your tempest" and "your storm" are the storm of God. The prayer is rendered as written.
- **83:16** "That they may seek your name, LORD" gives the purpose of the shame. The enemies are to come to know God.
- **83:17** "Let them perish in disgrace" is rendered as written, with no softening.
- **83:18** "Whose name is the LORD" translates *shimkha YHWH le-vaddekha*, "you whose name alone is YHWH". It is the closing verse of the group of psalms in which the divine name is mostly *Elohim* (Psalms 42 to 83). "The Most High" is *Elyon*.
- **84** In Psalms 84, 85, 88 and 89 the Hebrew heading is verse 1 (verses 1 and 2 in Psalm 88), so that English 84:1-12 is Hebrew 84:2-13, English 85:1-13 is Hebrew 85:2-14, English 88:1-18 is Hebrew 88:3-19, and English 89:1-52 is Hebrew 89:2-53. Psalms 86 and 87 are numbered alike. In the Greek and Latin numbering these psalms are one lower, from 83 to 88.
- **84:1** "How lovely is your dwelling place" translates *mah yedidot mishkenotekha*, in which the plural is a plural of majesty, and *yedidot* may mean "beloved" or "lovely".
- **84:2** "My soul" is *nafshi*. See entry H-NEPHESH. "Longs, yes, faints" translates *nikhsefah ve-gam-kalatah*. "The living God" translates *El chai*.
- **84:3** "Even the sparrow finds a home, and the swallow a nest for herself, where she may lay her young, at your altars" says that the birds nest in or near the temple. The Hebrew may be read "her young, your altars", so that the nest may be at the altars or the birds' young are the temple's.
- **84:5** "In whose heart are the highways to Zion" translates *mesillot bi-levavam*, in which "to Zion" is not in the Hebrew. The highways are the roads of pilgrimage.
- **84:6** "The Valley of Baca" translates *emeq ha-bakha*, which may mean "the valley of weeping" or "the valley of balsam trees". Its place is not known. "The early rain also covers it with pools" translates *gam-berakhot ya'teh moreh*, in which *berakhot* may mean "blessings" or "pools".
- **84:7** "From strength to strength" translates *me-chayil el-chayil*. "Each one appears before God in Zion" follows the Hebrew *yera'eh el-Elohim be-Tsiyyon*. The Greek has "the God of gods will be seen in Zion".
- **84:9** "Your anointed" is Hebrew *meshichekha*. See entry H-MASHIACH. It is the king.
- **84:10** "A doorkeeper" translates *histofef*, a rare verb that means "to stand at the threshold".
- **84:11** "A sun and shield" translates *shemesh u-magen*. It is the only place in the Old Testament where God is called a sun.
- **85:1** "You restored the fortunes of Jacob" translates *shavta shevut Ya'aqov*.
- **85:2** "You forgave the iniquity of your people. You covered all their sin" is as in 32:1.
- **85:4** "Restore us" translates *shuvenu*, "turn us back". See entry H-SHUV.
- **85:8** "Peace" is *shalom*. See entry H-SHALOM. "His faithful ones" translates *chasidav*. See entry H-HESED. "But let them not turn back to folly" translates *ve-al-yashuvu le-chislah*, which is hard and may be read "and not to those who turn to folly".
- **85:10** "Steadfast love and faithfulness meet. Righteousness and peace kiss each other" shows four qualities as persons. "Steadfast love" is *chesed*. "Righteousness" is *tsedeq*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **85:13** "Make his footsteps a way" translates *yasem le-derekh pe'amav*, which is hard and may be read "set his steps on the way".
- **86** "A prayer of David" is the one psalm of David in Book Three (Psalms 73 to 89). The word "prayer" is *tefillah* (see Psalm 17).
- **86:1** "Poor and needy" translates *ani ve-evyon*.
- **86:2** "I am faithful" translates *ki-chasid ani*. See entry H-HESED.
- **86:4** "Gladden the soul of your servant" uses *nefesh*. See entry H-NEPHESH. "To you, Lord, I lift up my soul" is as in 25:1.
- **86:5** "Good and forgiving" translates *tov ve-sallach*. "Steadfast love" is *chesed*. See entry H-HESED.
- **86:8** "There is none like you among the gods" translates *ein-kamokha va-elohim*.
- **86:9** "All the nations you have made will come" says that the nations will worship the LORD.
- **86:11** "Unite my heart to fear your name" translates *yached levavi le-yir'ah shemekha*. The Greek has "let my heart rejoice".
- **86:13** "The depths of Sheol" translates *Sheol tachtiyyah*. See entry H-SHEOL.
- **86:14** "Insolent people" translates *zedim*, as in 54:3, and "ruthless men" translates *aritsim*.
- **86:15** "A God merciful and gracious, slow to anger and abounding in steadfast love and faithfulness" repeats the words of the LORD in Exodus 34:6. The wording is kept matched with the WABV text of that verse.
- **86:16** "The son of your maidservant" translates *ben amatekha*, a term for a born servant (see Psalm 116:16).
- **86:17** "A sign of your favour" translates *ot le-tovah*, "a sign for good".
- **87:1** "On the holy mount stands the city he founded" translates *yesudato be-harerei qodesh*, literally "his foundation on holy mountains", which is incomplete in the Hebrew. See entry H-QADOSH. Psalm 87 is among the shortest and hardest psalms, and its text may be damaged.
- **87:3** "Glorious things are spoken of you" translates *nikhbadot medubbar bakh*.
- **87:4** "Rahab" is a name for Egypt (see Isaiah 30:7 and Psalm 89:10). "Among those who know me" is the voice of God. "This one was born there" says that people of these nations are counted as born in Zion.
- **87:5** "This one and that one were born in her" translates *ish ve-ish yullad bah*, "a man and a man were born in her". The Greek has "Mother Zion".
- **87:6** "The LORD records as he registers the peoples" translates *YHWH yispor bichtov ammim*, "the LORD counts in the register of peoples".
- **87:7** "Singers and dancers alike" translates *ve-sharim ke-cholelim*, in which *cholelim* may mean "dancers" or "flute players". "All my springs are in you" translates *kol-ma'yanai bakh*. The line is hard, and the speaker is not named.
- **88** "Mahalath Leannoth" names a tune, perhaps meaning "sickness, to afflict". "Heman the Ezrahite" was a wise man (see 1 Kings 4:31 and 1 Chronicles 2:6). The psalm is the one psalm of lament that has no turn to praise. It ends in darkness.
- **88:3** "My soul" is *nafshi*. See entry H-NEPHESH. "Sheol" is the place of the dead. See entry H-SHEOL.
- **88:4** "The pit" is *bor*, the grave. "A man who has no strength" translates *gever ein-eyal*.
- **88:5** "Like one set loose among the dead" translates *ba-metim chofshi*, in which *chofshi* means "free" and may mean "cast off" or "outcast". "Like those whom you remember no more" says that the dead are forgotten by God.
- **88:6** "In the regions dark and deep" translates *be-machashakkim bi-metsolot*.
- **88:8** "A horror" translates *to'evot*, "an abomination". "I am shut in" translates *kalu*.
- **88:9** "My eye grows dim through sorrow" translates *eini da'avah mi-oni*.
- **88:10** "The departed" translates *refa'im*, the shades of the dead. The questions in verses 10 to 12 expect the answer "no", and say that the dead do not praise God (see 6:5 and 30:9).
- **88:11** "Abaddon" is a name of the place of the dead, "destruction" (see Job 26:6 and 28:22).
- **88:12** "The land of forgetfulness" translates *eretz neshiyyah*.
- **88:14** "Why do you cast my soul away?" uses *nafshi*.
- **88:15** "Afflicted and close to death from my youth" translates *ani ani ve-gove'a mi-ne'ar*. "I am helpless" translates the rare word *afunah*, which may mean "numb" or "distraught".
- **88:16** "Your dreadful assaults" translates *bi'utekha*.
- **88:18** "My companions have become darkness" translates *meyuddai machshakh*, literally "my acquaintances, darkness". It is hard and may also be read "darkness is my closest friend" or "my acquaintances are in darkness". It is rendered as written, with no softening.
- **89** "Ethan the Ezrahite" was a wise man (see 1 Kings 4:31 and 1 Chronicles 2:6). The psalm moves from praise (verses 1 to 18), through the oracle of the covenant with David (verses 19 to 37), to the complaint that the LORD has broken it (verses 38 to 51).
- **89:1** "The steadfast love of the LORD" is *chasdei YHWH*. See entry H-HESED.
- **89:2** "Steadfast love will be built up for ever" translates *olam chesed yibbaneh*.
- **89:3** "A covenant" is Hebrew *berit*. See entry H-BERIT. "My chosen one" is *bechiri*. The verses 3 and 4 summarize the promise to David (see 2 Samuel 7:8-16 and 1 Chronicles 17:7-14), and the wording is kept matched with those passages where the Hebrew is the same.
- **89:5** "The assembly of the holy ones" translates *qehal qedoshim*. See entry H-QADOSH. The holy ones are the heavenly beings.
- **89:6** "The heavenly beings" translates *bivnei elim*, "among the sons of gods".
- **89:7** "The council of the holy ones" translates *sod-qedoshim*.
- **89:10** "Rahab" is the sea monster of the old stories (see Job 9:13, 26:12 and Isaiah 51:9). "Like a carcass" translates *ka-challal*.
- **89:12** "The north and the south" translates *tsafon ve-yamin*. "Tabor and Hermon" are the mountains of the west and the east.
- **89:14** "Righteousness and justice" are *tsedeq u-mishpat*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH. "The foundation of your throne" translates *makhon kis'ekha*.
- **89:15** "The festal shout" translates *teru'ah*.
- **89:17** "Our horn is exalted" is a figure of strength.
- **89:18** "Our shield belongs to the LORD, our king to the Holy One of Israel" may mean that the king is the LORD's shield.
- **89:19** "To your faithful one" follows the Greek and the Syriac. The written Hebrew has the plural, "to your faithful ones". "I have granted help to one who is mighty" translates *shivviti ezer al-gibbor*, in which *ezer* means "help" and may be read *nezer*, "crown".
- **89:20** "With my holy oil I have anointed him" refers to 1 Samuel 16:1-13.
- **89:22** "The enemy will not outwit him" translates *lo-yashi oyev bo*, in which the verb may mean "exact tribute" or "deceive".
- **89:25** "His hand on the sea and his right hand on the rivers" is a rule from the Mediterranean to the Euphrates.
- **89:26** "You are my Father" is as in 2 Samuel 7:14.
- **89:27** "The firstborn" is *bekhor*.
- **89:30** "My teaching" is *torati*. See entry H-TORAH.
- **89:32** "With the rod ... with stripes" is as in 2 Samuel 7:14.
- **89:35** "By my holiness" translates *be-qodshi*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **89:37** "My faithful witness in the skies" translates *ed ba-shachaq ne'eman*, which may refer to the moon, to the sun, or to the rainbow.
- **89:38** "Your anointed" is Hebrew *meshichekha*. See entry H-MASHIACH. The verses 38 to 45 are the complaint that God has broken the covenant. They are rendered as written, with no softening.
- **89:39** "You have renounced the covenant" translates *na'artah berit*.
- **89:44** "You have made his splendour cease" translates *hishbata mi-tohoro*, in which *tohar* is a rare word that may mean "splendour" or "purity".
- **89:47** "Remember how short my time is" translates *zekhor-ani mah-cheled*, in which *cheled* is the span of life.
- **89:48** "Sheol" is the place of the dead. See entry H-SHEOL.
- **89:51** "The footsteps of your anointed" translates *iqqevot meshichekha*, "the heels of your anointed", which may mean "the steps" or "the delays".
- **89:52** "Blessed be the LORD for ever. Amen and Amen" is the closing blessing of Book Three. It is not part of the psalm (see also 41:13, 72:18-19 and 106:48).
- **90** Book Four (Psalms 90 to 106) begins here. In Psalm 92 the Hebrew heading is verse 1, so that English 92:1-15 is Hebrew 92:2-16. Psalms 90, 91 and 93 to 100 are numbered alike in Hebrew and English. In the Greek and Latin numbering these psalms are one lower, from 89 to 99. "A prayer of Moses, the man of God" is the only psalm headed with the name of Moses. "The man of God" is a title of Moses (see Deuteronomy 33:1).
- **90:1** "Our dwelling place" translates *ma'on*, a place of shelter.
- **90:2** "Before the mountains were brought forth, or ever you had formed the earth" uses birth imagery (the verbs *yulladu* and *techolel*). "From everlasting to everlasting" translates *me-olam ad-olam*.
- **90:3** "You return human beings to dust" recalls Genesis 3:19. "Return, children of men" uses *shuvu*. See entry H-SHUV.
- **90:4** "A thousand years in your sight are like yesterday" is quoted in 2 Peter 3:8. "A watch in the night" is one of the divisions of the night.
- **90:5** "You sweep them away like a flood. They are like a dream" translates *zeramtam shenah yihyu*, which is hard and may be read "you flood them; they are sleep". "Renewed" translates *yachalof*, which may mean "sprouts" or "passes away".
- **90:8** "Our secret sins" translates *alumenu*, "our hidden things".
- **90:9** "Like a sigh" translates *ke-hegeh*, which may mean "a murmur" or "a breath".
- **90:10** "Their span" translates *rahbam*, which may mean "their pride" or "their span". "Toil and trouble" is *amal va-aven*.
- **90:11** "Who considers the power of your anger, and your wrath according to the fear of you?" is hard in the Hebrew.
- **90:12** "A heart of wisdom" translates *levav chokhmah*.
- **90:13** "Return, LORD" translates *shuvah YHWH*. "Have pity" translates *hinnachem*. See entry H-NACHAM.
- **90:14** "Your steadfast love" is *chasdekha*. See entry H-HESED.
- **90:17** "The favour of the Lord our God" translates *no'am Adonai Eloheinu*. "Establish the work of our hands" is repeated.
- **91** Psalm 91 has no heading in the Hebrew. The Greek calls it a psalm of David. The speaker changes: verses 1 and 2 are the poet's, verses 3 to 13 are addressed to the person who trusts, and verses 14 to 16 are the voice of God.
- **91:1** "The Most High" is *Elyon* and "the Almighty" is *Shaddai*. "The shelter" translates *seter* and "the shadow" is *tsel*.
- **91:2** "I will say to the LORD" follows the Hebrew *omar*. The Greek has "he will say".
- **91:3** "The snare of the fowler" translates *pach yaqush*. "The deadly pestilence" translates *devar havvot*.
- **91:4** "A shield and buckler" translates *tsinnah ve-socherah*, in which *socherah* is a rare word.
- **91:6** "The destruction that wastes at noonday" translates *qetev yashud tsohorayim*. The word *qetev* was later taken as the name of a demon.
- **91:7** "A thousand may fall at your side" says that the person who trusts is kept from the plague.
- **91:10** "No evil will be allowed to befall you" translates *lo-te'unneh elekha ra'ah*. "Plague" is *nega*.
- **91:11** The verses 11 and 12 are quoted in Matthew 4:6 and Luke 4:10-11, where the quotation is shorter than the psalm and leaves out the words "in all your ways".
- **91:13** "The lion and the adder" translates *shachal va-fethen*, and "the young lion and the serpent" is *kefir ve-tannin*.
- **91:14** "Holds fast to me in love" translates *bi chashaq*. "I will protect him" translates *asaggevehu*, "I will set him on high".
- **92** "A song for the Sabbath day" is the only psalm with the Sabbath in its heading.
- **92:2** "Your steadfast love" is *chasdekha*. See entry H-HESED.
- **92:3** "The lute" translates *nevel* and "the lyre" is *kinnor*. "The melody" translates *higgayon*, which may mean "a resounding".
- **92:4** "Have made me glad" translates *simmachtani*.
- **92:6** "The senseless" translates *ish ba'ar*, and "the fool" is *kesil*.
- **92:7** "They are doomed to destruction for ever" translates *le-hishshamdam adei-ad*.
- **92:10** "Like that of the wild ox" translates *ka-re'em*. "You have poured fresh oil over me" translates *balloti be-shemen ra'anan*, in which the verb *balloti* is rare and may mean "I am anointed" or "I am mingled".
- **92:11** "My eyes have seen the downfall of my enemies" translates *va-tabbet eini be-shurai*, which has no word for "downfall" in the Hebrew. "My evil assailants" translates *ba-qamim alai mere'im*.
- **92:13** "They are planted in the house of the LORD" refers to the temple trees.
- **92:14** "Full of sap and green" translates *deshenim ve-ra'ananim*.
- **92:15** "There is no unrighteousness in him" follows the reading tradition (*avlatah*). The written text has *olatah*.
- **93** Psalm 93 has no heading in the Hebrew. The Greek has "for the day before the Sabbath, when the earth was inhabited". Psalms 93 and 95 to 99 are the psalms of the kingship of the LORD.
- **93:1** "The LORD reigns" translates *YHWH malakh*, which may also be read "the LORD has become king". "Majesty" is *ge'ut*. "It will never be moved" says that the world is secure because of the LORD's rule.
- **93:3** "The floods" translates *neharot*, "rivers", the powers of the sea. "Their roaring" translates *dokhyam*, a rare word that may mean "their pounding".
- **93:4** "Mightier than the thunders of many waters, mightier than the waves of the sea" says that the LORD is stronger than the powers of chaos.
- **93:5** "Your decrees" translates *edotekha*. "Holiness befits your house" translates *le-vetkha na'avah-qodesh*, in which *na'avah* is a rare word that may mean "befits" or "is the beauty of". See entry H-QADOSH. "For evermore" translates *le-orekh yamim*, "for length of days".
- **94:1** "God of vengeance" translates *El nequmot*. The vengeance in this psalm is justice, the act of the judge of the earth against the oppressors (see 94:2). The prayer is rendered as written, with no softening. "Shine forth" translates *hofia*.
- **94:2** "Judge of the earth" translates *shofet ha-arets*.
- **94:6** "The widow and the stranger and the fatherless" are the three groups for whom the law asks care.
- **94:7** "The LORD does not see" is the thought of the wicked (see 10:11 and 73:11).
- **94:8** "The dullest of the people" translates *ba'arim ba-am*.
- **94:9** "He who planted the ear, does he not hear?" is an argument from creation.
- **94:11** "A breath" is *hevel*.
- **94:12** "Whom you discipline" translates *te-yasserennu*, from *musar*. "Your teaching" translates *mi-toratekha*. See entry H-TORAH.
- **94:13** "Until a pit is dug for the wicked" says that the wicked will fall.
- **94:15** "Justice will return to the righteous" translates *ki-ad-tsedeq yashuv mishpat*, which is hard and may be read "judgement will return to righteousness".
- **94:17** "My soul" is *nafshi*. See entry H-NEPHESH. "The land of silence" translates *dumah*, a name of Sheol.
- **94:18** "Your steadfast love" is *chasdekha*. See entry H-HESED.
- **94:19** "The cares of my heart" translates *sar'appai*, a rare word that means "anxious thoughts".
- **94:20** "Wicked rulers" translates *kisse havvot*, "a throne of destruction". "Who frame injustice by statute" translates *yotser amal alei choq*.
- **94:23** "Wipe them out" translates *yatsmitem*. The prayer is rendered as written.
- **95** Psalm 95 has no heading in the Hebrew. The Greek calls it a praise of a song of David. The verses 7 to 11 are quoted in Hebrews 3:7 to 4:11.
- **95:1** "Shout for joy" translates *nari'ah*. "The rock of our salvation" is *tsur yish'enu*.
- **95:3** "A great King above all gods" translates *melekh gadol al-kol-elohim*.
- **95:4** "The heights of the mountains" translates *to'afot harim*, in which *to'afot* is a rare word that may mean "the heights" or "the treasures".
- **95:6** "Worship" translates *nishtachaveh*, "bow down", and "kneel" is *nikhra'ah*.
- **95:7** "Today, if you hear his voice" begins the warning that is quoted in Hebrews 3:7.
- **95:8** "Meribah" and "Massah" refer to the stories of Exodus 17:1-7 and Numbers 20:1-13.
- **95:10** "I loathed that generation" translates *aqut be-dor*. "They have not known my ways" is the charge against the people.
- **95:11** "They will not enter my rest" translates *im-yevo'un el-menuchati*, an oath form (see Numbers 14:21-23 and Deuteronomy 12:9). The Greek of Hebrews 3:11 and 4:3 keeps the oath.
- **96** The verses 1 to 13 are nearly the same as 1 Chronicles 16:23-33, and the wording of the two is kept matched where the Hebrew is the same. The Greek has a heading for the psalm. The Hebrew has none.
- **96:1** "A new song" is as in 33:3, 98:1 and Isaiah 42:10.
- **96:2** "Tell of his salvation" translates *basseru mi-yom le-yom yeshu'ato*, in which the verb *basser* means "announce good news".
- **96:5** "Worthless idols" translates *elilim*, "nothings", which is a play on the word *elohim*, "gods".
- **96:6** "Splendour and majesty" are *hod ve-hadar*. "His sanctuary" is *miqdasho*. The text of 1 Chronicles 16:27 differs ("glory and joy are in his place").
- **96:9** "The splendour of holiness" translates *be-hadrat-qodesh*, as in 29:2. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **96:10** "The world is established. It will never be moved" is as in 93:1.
- **96:13** "For he comes" is repeated. "In righteousness" translates *be-tsedeq*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **97** Psalm 97 has no heading in the Hebrew. The Greek has "of David, when his land was restored".
- **97:1** "The coastlands" translates *iyyim*, the far lands of the west.
- **97:2** "Clouds and thick darkness" translates *anan va-arafel* and recalls Sinai (Deuteronomy 4:11). "Righteousness and justice are the foundation of his throne" is as in 89:14. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **97:3** "Fire goes before him" is a figure of the appearance of God in the storm (see 18:8).
- **97:7** "Worthless idols" translates *elilim* (see 96:5). "Worship him, all you gods" translates *hishtachavu-lo kol-elohim*, in which the gods are the heavenly beings. The Greek has "all his angels", which is quoted in Hebrews 1:6.
- **97:8** "The daughters of Judah" are the towns of Judah (see 48:11).
- **97:9** "Most high" translates *elyon*.
- **97:10** "You who love the LORD, hate evil" follows the Hebrew *ohavei YHWH sin'u ra*. "His faithful ones" translates *chasidav*. See entry H-HESED.
- **97:11** "Light is sown for the righteous" follows the Hebrew *or zarua la-tsaddiq*, "light is sown". The Greek has "light has risen" (*zarach*), which may be the original reading.
- **97:12** "His holy name" translates *zekher qodsho*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **98:1** "A new song" is as in 96:1. "His holy arm" translates *zero'a qodsho*. See entry H-QADOSH. "Have won salvation for him" translates *hoshi'ah-lo*, "has saved for himself".
- **98:2** "He has revealed his righteousness in the sight of the nations" is the central statement of the psalm (see Isaiah 52:10).
- **98:3** "His steadfast love" is *chasdo*. See entry H-HESED.
- **98:6** "Trumpets" translates *chatsotserot*, and "the horn" is *shofar*.
- **98:7** The verses 7 to 9 are nearly the same as 96:11-13.
- **98:9** "With righteousness" translates *be-tsedeq*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **99** Psalm 99 has no heading in the Hebrew. It has the refrain "Holy is he" (verses 3, 5 and 9).
- **99:1** "Sits enthroned on the cherubim" refers to the ark (see 80:1 and 1 Samuel 4:4). "Let the earth quake" translates *tanut ha-arets*.
- **99:3** "Holy is he" translates *qadosh hu*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **99:4** "The King in his might loves justice" translates *oz melekh mishpat ahev*, which is hard and may be read "the strength of the King loves justice".
- **99:5** "His footstool" translates *hadom raglav*, which is the ark or the temple (see 132:7 and 1 Chronicles 28:2).
- **99:6** "Moses and Aaron were among his priests, Samuel also was among those who called on his name" names three leaders who spoke for the people. Moses was a priest in the early service (Exodus 24:6-8), and Samuel offered sacrifices.
- **99:8** "You were a forgiving God to them, but an avenger of their wrongdoings" translates *El nosse hayita lahem ve-noqem al-alilotam*, in which the second half may be read "an avenger of their deeds". It says that God both forgave and punished (see Numbers 14:20-23 and 20:12).
- **99:9** "His holy mountain" translates *har qodsho*.
- **100** "A psalm for giving thanks" translates *mizmor le-todah*, a psalm of the thank offering.
- **100:1** "Shout for joy" translates *hari'u*.
- **100:3** "We are his" follows the reading tradition (*ve-lo anachnu*, "his are we", with *lo* meaning "to him"). The written text has *lo* meaning "not", so that it reads *ve-lo anachnu*, which may be read "and not we ourselves". The Greek has "and not we ourselves". The WABV text takes "we are his".
- **100:5** "His steadfast love" is *chasdo*. See entry H-HESED.
- **101** In Psalm 102 the Hebrew heading is verse 1, so that English 102:1-28 is Hebrew 102:2-29. Psalms 101 and 103 to 106 are numbered alike in Hebrew and English. In the Greek and Latin numbering these psalms are one lower, from 100 to 105. Psalm 101 is the vow of a king to rule justly.
- **101:1** "Steadfast love and justice" translates *chesed u-mishpat*. See entry H-HESED.
- **101:2** "I will ponder the way that is blameless" translates *askilah be-derekh tamim*. "When will you come to me?" is a question to God. "Within my house" translates *be-qerev beti*.
- **101:3** "Anything that is worthless" translates *davar beliya'al*. "The work of those who fall away" translates *asoh setim*, in which *setim* is a rare word for those who turn aside.
- **101:4** "A perverse heart" translates *levav iqqesh*.
- **101:5** "Whoever slanders his neighbour in secret I will destroy" translates *melashni ba-seter re'ehu otso atsmit*. "I will not endure" translates *oto lo uchal*, which is hard and may be read "I cannot bear him".
- **101:6** "The faithful in the land" translates *ne'emenei erets*.
- **101:8** "Morning by morning I will destroy all the wicked in the land" is the king's vow, rendered as written. It is a promise of justice in the courts and not of killing in every case. "The city of the LORD" is Jerusalem.
- **102** "A prayer of one afflicted" translates *tefillah le-ani*. "When he is faint" translates *ki ya'atof*. "Pours out his complaint" translates *yishpokh sicho*. The psalm is one of the seven penitential psalms of later tradition.
- **102:3** "My bones burn like a furnace" translates *atsmotai ke-moqed nicharu*, in which *moqed* means "a hearth".
- **102:4** "I forget to eat my bread" says that he has lost his appetite.
- **102:6** "A desert owl" translates *qa'at midbar* and "an owl of the waste places" is *kos charavot*. The kinds of the birds are not certain.
- **102:7** "A lonely sparrow" translates *tsippor boded*.
- **102:8** "Use my name for a curse" translates *nishba'u vi*, "swear by me", so that the poet's name has become an oath of ill luck.
- **102:12** "Enthroned for ever" translates *le-olam teshev*.
- **102:13** "The appointed time" translates *mo'ed*.
- **102:17** "The destitute" translates *ha-ar'ar*, a rare word.
- **102:18** "A people yet to be created" translates *am nivra*.
- **102:19** "His holy height" translates *mi-merom qodsho*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **102:20** "Those who were doomed to die" translates *benei temutah*, "sons of death".
- **102:23** "He has broken my strength in midcourse" translates *innah va-derekh kochi*, in which the reading tradition has "my strength" and the written text has "his strength". The line is hard.
- **102:25** The verses 25 to 27 are quoted in Hebrews 1:10-12, where the Greek has "Lord" and applies the words to the Son.
- **102:27** "You are the same" translates *attah hu*.
- **103:1** "Bless the LORD, my soul" translates *barekhi nafshi et-YHWH*. See entry H-NEPHESH. "All that is within me" translates *kol-qeravai*, "all my inward parts". "His holy name" translates *shem qodsho*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **103:3** "Who forgives" translates *ha-solei'ach* and "who heals" translates *ha-rofe*.
- **103:4** "Who redeems your life from the pit" translates *ha-go'el mi-shachat chayyekhi*. See entry H-GOEL. "Steadfast love" is *chesed*. See entry H-HESED.
- **103:5** "Who satisfies you with good" translates *ha-masbi'a ba-tov edyekh*, in which *edyekh* is a hard word that may mean "your mouth", "your ornament" or "your years". "Your youth is renewed like the eagle's" is a figure of strength.
- **103:6** "Righteousness" is *tsedaqot*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **103:7** "He made known his ways to Moses" recalls Exodus 33:13 and 34:6-7.
- **103:8** "Merciful and gracious, slow to anger and abounding in steadfast love" repeats the words of Exodus 34:6, and the wording is kept matched with the WABV text of that verse.
- **103:9** "He will not always accuse" translates *lo la-netsach yarib*.
- **103:13** "Shows compassion" translates *racham*.
- **103:14** "Our frame" translates *yitsrenu*, "what we are formed of".
- **103:15** "Their days are like grass" is as in Isaiah 40:6-8.
- **103:18** "His covenant" is *beritho*. See entry H-BERIT.
- **103:20** "His angels" translates *mal'akhav*, and "you mighty ones" translates *gibborei koach*.
- **104** Psalm 104 has no heading in the Hebrew. The Greek calls it a psalm of David. It has close links with Genesis 1 and with an Egyptian hymn to the sun of the fourteenth century BC, which the footnotes do not discuss further.
- **104:1** "Bless the LORD, my soul" uses *nafshi*. See entry H-NEPHESH. "Splendour and majesty" translates *hod ve-hadar*.
- **104:2** "Covering yourself with light as with a garment" translates *oteh-or ka-salmah*. "Like a tent" translates *ka-yeri'ah*.
- **104:3** "He lays the beams of his chambers on the waters" refers to the upper waters above the heavens (see Genesis 1:7).
- **104:4** "He makes his messengers winds" translates *oseh mal'akhav ruchot*, which may also be read "he makes the winds his messengers". The Greek is quoted in Hebrews 1:7 ("who makes his angels winds").
- **104:5** "He set the earth on its foundations" is as in Job 38:4-6.
- **104:6** "You covered it with the deep as with a garment" refers to the waters of the first day.
- **104:8** "The mountains rose, the valleys sank down" translates *ya'alu harim yeredu be-qa'ot*, which may also be read of the waters ("they went up over the mountains, they went down into the valleys").
- **104:9** "You set a boundary that they may not pass" recalls the promise after the flood (Genesis 9:11-15).
- **104:11** "The wild donkeys" translates *pera'im*.
- **104:12** "Among the branches" translates *mi-ben ofayim*.
- **104:13** "Your lofty abode" translates *aliyyotav*, "his upper chambers".
- **104:14** "Plants for people to cultivate" translates *esev la-avodat ha-adam*.
- **104:15** "Oil to make the face shine" translates *le-hatsil panim mi-shamen*, which is hard and may be read "to make the face shine more than oil".
- **104:16** "The trees of the LORD" translates *atsei YHWH*.
- **104:17** "The stork" translates *chasidah* (see Job 39:13). "The fir trees" translates *berosh*.
- **104:18** "The wild goats" translates *ya'alim* and "the rock badgers" are *shefannim*.
- **104:21** "Seeking their food from God" says that even the lions depend on God.
- **104:24** "In wisdom you have made them all" translates *kullam be-chokhmah asita*, as in Proverbs 3:19.
- **104:26** "Leviathan, which you formed to play in it" translates *livyatan zeh-yatsarta le-sachaq-bo*. The sea monster is a creature that plays (see Job 41 and Psalm 74:14).
- **104:29** "Their breath" translates *ruchatam*. See entry H-RUACH. The Hebrew word *ruach* means "wind", "breath" and "spirit" (see Genesis 2:7 and Job 34:14-15).
- **104:30** "When you send out your breath" uses *ruach* again, and may also be read "your Spirit". The WABV text gives "breath" in both verses. "They are created" uses *bara*.
- **104:32** "Who touches the mountains, and they smoke" refers to the appearance at Sinai (Exodus 19:18).
- **104:33** "While I have being" translates *be-odi*.
- **104:35** "Let sinners be consumed from the earth" is rendered as written. "Praise the LORD" translates *halelu-Yah*, the first occurrence of the word in the book of Psalms. "Yah" is the short form of *YHWH*.
- **105** Psalm 105 has no heading in the Hebrew. The verses 1 to 15 are nearly the same as 1 Chronicles 16:8-22, and the wording is kept matched where the Hebrew is the same.
- **105:3** "Glory in his holy name" translates *hit'halelu be-shem qodsho*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **105:5** "The judgements he uttered" translates *mishpetei-fiv*, "the judgements of his mouth".
- **105:6** "Offspring of Abraham ... children of Jacob" follows the Hebrew. 1 Chronicles 16:13 has "offspring of Israel".
- **105:8** "His covenant" is *beritho*. See entry H-BERIT. "For a thousand generations" is as in Deuteronomy 7:9.
- **105:12** "Few in number" translates *metei mispar*.
- **105:15** "My anointed ones" translates *meshichai*, which names the patriarchs. See entry H-MASHIACH.
- **105:17** "Joseph, who was sold as a slave" translates *le-eved nimkar Yosef*.
- **105:18** "His neck was put in a collar of iron" translates *barzel ba'ah nafsho*, literally "iron came into his soul" (*nefesh*, which may also be "neck").
- **105:19** "The word of the LORD tested him" translates *imrat YHWH tseraftehu*. The word may be the LORD's or Joseph's own word.
- **105:22** "To instruct his princes as he pleased" follows the Greek and the Syriac (*le-yasser*). The Hebrew has "to bind his princes at his pleasure" (*le-esor*). The WABV text departs from the Hebrew reading and is flagged for the scholar.
- **105:25** "He turned their hearts to hate his people" is rendered as written.
- **105:27** "They performed his signs among them" refers to Moses and Aaron.
- **105:28** "They did not rebel against his words" translates *ve-lo-marru et-devarav*. The Greek has "they rebelled", and the line is hard. The darkness is put first in the list of plagues, in a different order from Exodus.
- **105:31** "Gnats" translates *kinnim*.
- **105:36** "The firstfruits of all their strength" translates *reshit le-khol-onam*.
- **105:42** "His holy promise" translates *devar qodsho*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **105:45** "His teachings" translates *torotav*. See entry H-TORAH. The land was given so that they would keep his law.
- **106** Psalm 106 has no heading in the Hebrew. It opens and closes with "Praise the LORD" (*halelu-Yah*). It is a confession of the sins of the people from Egypt to the exile. It does not follow the order of the books of Exodus and Numbers in every point, and it does not mention Korah by name (see 106:17).
- **106:1** "His steadfast love endures for ever" translates *ki le-olam chasdo*, as in 1 Chronicles 16:34. See entry H-HESED.
- **106:3** "Do righteousness at all times" translates *oseh tsedaqah be-khol-et*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH. The verb is singular in the Hebrew.
- **106:4** "Remember me, LORD" is the voice of one person within the people's prayer.
- **106:5** "Your chosen ones" translates *bechirekha*, "your nation" is *goyekha*, and "your inheritance" is *nachalatekha*.
- **106:6** "Both we and our ancestors have sinned" translates *chatanu im-avotenu*. The three verbs are "sinned", "committed iniquity" (*he'evinu*) and "done wickedly" (*hirsha'nu*).
- **106:7** "They rebelled at the sea" refers to Exodus 14:11-12.
- **106:9** "The Red Sea" translates *yam suf*, "the Sea of Reeds".
- **106:12** "Then they believed his words" recalls Exodus 14:31.
- **106:14** "A wanton craving" translates *va-yit'avvu ta'avah*, as in Numbers 11:4.
- **106:15** "A wasting disease" translates *razon*, a rare word that may mean "leanness" or "wasting" (see Numbers 11:33).
- **106:16** "Aaron, the holy one of the LORD" translates *Aharon qedosh YHWH*. See entry H-QADOSH. The story is in Numbers 16.
- **106:17** "The company of Abiram" refers to the rebels of Numbers 16. Korah is not named.
- **106:19** "A calf in Horeb" refers to Exodus 32.
- **106:20** "They exchanged their glory for the image of an ox that eats grass" follows the Hebrew *kevodam*. A scribal tradition says that the original read *kevodo*, "his glory", that is the glory of God (see Jeremiah 2:11).
- **106:23** "Stood in the breach" translates *amad ba-peretz*, a figure of a person who stands in the gap of a wall. It refers to Exodus 32:11-14.
- **106:24** "The pleasant land" translates *erets chemdah*. The story is in Numbers 13 and 14.
- **106:26** "He raised his hand and swore" refers to Numbers 14:28-35. "Make them fall" translates *le-happil*.
- **106:28** "The Baal of Peor" refers to Numbers 25. "Sacrifices offered to the dead" translates *zivchei metim*, which may refer to sacrifices to dead idols or to the dead.
- **106:30** "Intervened" translates *va-yefalel*, in which the verb may mean "intervened", "prayed" or "executed judgement". The psalm does not say how he did it (see Numbers 25:7-8).
- **106:31** "That was counted to him as righteousness" translates *va-techashev lo li-tsedaqah*, as in Genesis 15:6. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **106:32** "The waters of Meribah" refers to Numbers 20:2-13.
- **106:33** "They made his spirit bitter" translates *himru et-ruchoh*, in which "his" may be Moses's or God's. See entry H-RUACH.
- **106:34** "They did not destroy the peoples" refers to Judges 1 and 2.
- **106:37** "They sacrificed their sons and their daughters to the demons" translates *va-yizbechu et-baneihem ve-et-benoteihem la-shedim*. It is rendered as written, with no softening. The *shedim* are spirits or false gods. The practice is forbidden in Deuteronomy 12:31 and is described in 2 Kings 17:17.
- **106:38** "The land was polluted with blood" translates *va-techenaf ha-arets ba-damim*, as in Numbers 35:33.
- **106:39** "Played the whore in their deeds" translates *va-yiznu be-ma'aseihem*, a figure of unfaithfulness.
- **106:43** "Rebellious in their purposes" translates *yamru ba-atsatam*.
- **106:45** "His covenant" is *beritho*. See entry H-BERIT. "Relented" translates *va-yinnachem*. See entry H-NACHAM.
- **106:46** "He caused them to be pitied by all those who held them captive" refers to the exile (see 1 Kings 8:50).
- **106:47** "Gather us from among the nations" shows the people as scattered. "Your holy name" translates *shem qodshekha*. The verses 47 and 48 are nearly the same as 1 Chronicles 16:35-36.
- **106:48** "Blessed be the LORD, the God of Israel, from everlasting to everlasting. And let all the people say, 'Amen.' Praise the LORD" is the closing blessing of Book Four. It is not part of the psalm (see also 41:13, 72:18-19 and 89:52).
- **107** Book Five (Psalms 107 to 150) begins here. In Psalms 108 and 109 the Hebrew heading is verse 1, so that English 108:1-13 is Hebrew 108:2-14 and English 109:1-31 is Hebrew 109:2-32. Psalms 107 and 110 to 118 are numbered alike in Hebrew and English. In the Greek and Latin numbering Psalms 107 to 113 are one lower (106 to 112). The Greek joins Psalms 114 and 115 as one psalm (113), divides Psalm 116 into two (114 and 115), and so Psalm 117 is 116 and Psalm 118 is 117. Psalm 107 has no heading in the Hebrew. It tells of four groups of people who are rescued (the travellers in the desert, the prisoners, the sick and the sailors), each with the refrain "Then they cried to the LORD in their trouble" (verses 6, 13, 19 and 28) and "Let them thank the LORD" (verses 8, 15, 21 and 31).
- **107:1** "His steadfast love endures for ever" translates *ki le-olam chasdo*, as in 106:1. See entry H-HESED.
- **107:2** "The redeemed of the LORD" translates *ge'ulei YHWH*. See entry H-GOEL.
- **107:3** "From the north and from the south" translates *mi-tsafon u-mi-yam*, literally "from the north and from the sea", the sea being the west or south. Some read "from the south" (*mi-yamin*).
- **107:5** "Their soul fainted within them" uses *nefesh*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **107:10** "In darkness and in deep darkness" translates *choshekh ve-tsalmavet*.
- **107:11** "They had rebelled against the words of God and spurned the counsel of the Most High" gives the cause of the prisoners' state.
- **107:12** "With hard labour" translates *be-amal*.
- **107:16** "The doors of bronze" and "the bars of iron" are the gates of the prison (see Isaiah 45:2).
- **107:17** "Fools" translates *evilim*. "Through their sinful ways" translates *mi-derekh pish'am*, "because of the way of their transgression".
- **107:18** "The gates of death" are as in 9:13.
- **107:20** "He sent out his word and healed them" shows the word as a messenger.
- **107:22** "Sacrifices of thanksgiving" translates *zivchei todah*.
- **107:23** "Doing business" translates *osei melakhah*, "doing work".
- **107:25** "The stormy wind" translates *ruach se'arah*.
- **107:26** "Their courage melted away" translates *nafsham be-ra'ah titmogag*, literally "their soul melted in the evil".
- **107:27** "They were at their wits' end" translates *va-chokhmatam titballa*, "all their wisdom was swallowed up".
- **107:29** "He made the storm be still" translates *yaqem se'arah li-dmamah*.
- **107:30** "Their desired haven" translates *mechoz chefatsam*.
- **107:32** "The assembly of the elders" translates *moshav zeqenim*.
- **107:33** The verses 33 to 42 turn from the rescues to the way that God changes the land and the lot of people.
- **107:40** "A pathless waste" translates *tohu lo-derekh*, as in Job 12:24.
- **107:41** "Makes their families like flocks" is a figure of large families.
- **107:42** "All wickedness shuts its mouth" is as in Job 5:16.
- **107:43** "Whoever is wise, let them attend to these things" is a wisdom ending. "The steadfast love of the LORD" is *chasdei YHWH*.
- **108** Psalm 108 is made of 57:7-11 (verses 1 to 5) and 60:5-12 (verses 6 to 13), and the WABV text keeps the wording matched where the Hebrew is the same. The differences are these. In 108:1 the line "my heart is steadfast" is not repeated, and the words "with all my glory" (*af kevodi*) are added. In 108:2 the words "my glory" are not there (57:8 has "Awake, my glory"). In 108:4 the steadfast love is "great above the heavens", where 57:10 has "great to the heavens". In 108:6 the line is "answer me" (*va-aneni*), where 60:5 has "answer us". In 108:9 "over Philistia I shout in triumph" differs from 60:8, where the Hebrew has "Philistia, shout because of me".
- **108:4** "Your steadfast love" is *chasdekha*. See entry H-HESED.
- **108:7** "In his holiness" translates *be-qodsho*, which may also be read "in his sanctuary". See entry H-QADOSH.
- **109** "God of my praise" translates *Elohei tehillati*. Psalm 109 is the hardest of the prayers against enemies in the book, and the verses 6 to 19 are the centre of it.
- **109:2** "Wicked and deceitful mouths" translates *pi rasha u-fi mirmah*, literally "the mouth of the wicked and the mouth of deceit".
- **109:4** "I am a man of prayer" translates *va-ani tefillah*, literally "but I am prayer".
- **109:6** "An accuser" translates *satan*, the word for an adversary in a court (see Zechariah 3:1 and 71:13). Here it is a human accuser. The word has no definite article. See entry H-SATAN. "At his right hand" is the place of the accuser in court (see verse 31, where the LORD stands at the right hand of the needy one).
- **109:6** The verses 6 to 19 speak of one man ("he"), while the other verses speak of many enemies. Some scholars think that the verses 6 to 19 are the words of the poet's accusers against him, which he quotes ("they say, 'Appoint a wicked man against him ...'"), and that verse 20 is his reply. The WABV text gives the verses as the poet's own prayer, as the Hebrew has them, and does not add a word to mark a quotation.
- **109:7** "Let his prayer be counted as sin" translates *u-tefillato tihyeh la-chata'ah*.
- **109:8** "May another take his office" translates *pequddato yiqqach acher*. It is quoted in Acts 1:20 in the Greek form, "his office of overseer".
- **109:9** The verses 9 to 15 pray that the man's children and wife and name be cut off. They are rendered as written, with no softening. They stand against Deuteronomy 24:16 and Ezekiel 18, where children are not punished for their fathers.
- **109:16** "For he did not remember to show steadfast love" gives the reason for the curses. See entry H-HESED.
- **109:18** "He clothed himself with cursing as his coat" is a figure of the habit of cursing.
- **109:20** "My accusers" translates *sotenai*, from the root *satan*. "Against my soul" uses *nafshi*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **109:21** "Act for me" translates *aseh itti*. "Your steadfast love is good" translates *ki tov chasdekha*.
- **109:23** "Like a shadow at evening" translates *ke-tselo ki-nototo*, "like a shadow when it lengthens".
- **109:24** "My body has become gaunt, with no fat" translates *u-vesari kachash mi-shamen*.
- **109:28** "Let them curse, but you will bless" is the poet's turn to trust.
- **109:31** "At the right hand of the needy one" is the answer to verse 6. "Those who condemn his soul to death" uses *nafsho*.
- **110** Psalm 110 is the psalm most often quoted in the New Testament (see Matthew 22:44, Mark 12:36, Luke 20:42-43, Acts 2:34-35, 1 Corinthians 15:25 and Hebrews 1:13 and 5:6).
- **110:1** "The LORD says to my lord" translates *ne'um YHWH la-adoni*. The word *ne'um* is the word for an oracle. "My lord" is *adoni*, a form of the word for a human lord, and not the divine title *Adonai*. "Your footstool" translates *hadom le-raglekha*.
- **110:2** "Your mighty sceptre" translates *matteh uzzekha*.
- **110:3** The verse is among the hardest in the book. "Your people will offer themselves freely" translates *ammekha nedavot*. "On the day of your power" translates *be-yom chelekha*, which may also mean "on the day of your army". "In holy splendour" translates *be-hadrei-qodesh*, which may also be read *be-harrei-qodesh*, "on the holy mountains". See entry H-QADOSH. "From the womb of the morning, the dew of your youth will be yours" translates *me-rechem mi-shachar lekha tal yaldutekha*, which is very hard. The Greek has "from the womb before the morning star I have begotten you".
- **110:4** "Will not change his mind" translates *lo yinnachem*. See entry H-NACHAM. "After the order of Melchizedek" translates *al-divrati Malki-tsedeq*, in which *al-divrati* may mean "in the manner of", "after the order of" or "because of the word of". The figure of Melchizedek is in Genesis 14:18-20, and the verse is quoted in Hebrews 5:6 and 7:17.
- **110:5** "The Lord is at your right hand" translates *Adonai al-yeminekha*. The speaker now addresses the king, and the LORD is at his side.
- **110:6** "Filling them with corpses" translates *male geviyyot*, which is hard and may be read "he fills the places with corpses". The line is rendered as written. "He will shatter chiefs over the wide earth" translates *machats rosh al-erets rabbah*, in which *rosh* may mean "head" or "chief".
- **110:7** "He will drink from the brook by the way. Therefore he will lift up his head" is hard and may mean that the king is refreshed after the battle.
- **111** Psalms 111 and 112 are acrostics, in which each half-line begins with a letter of the Hebrew alphabet in turn. The WABV text does not try to reproduce them. Neither psalm has a heading in the Hebrew. Both begin "Praise the LORD" (*halelu-Yah*).
- **111:1** "In the company of the upright, in the congregation" translates *be-sod yesharim ve-edah*.
- **111:2** "Studied by all who delight in them" translates *derushim le-khol-chafetsehem*, "sought out by all who delight in them".
- **111:3** "Splendour and majesty" translates *hod ve-hadar*. "His righteousness" is *tsidqato*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **111:4** "He has made his wonderful works to be remembered" translates *zekher asah le-nifle'otav*.
- **111:5** "Food" translates *teref*, a word that also means "prey". "His covenant" is *beritho*. See entry H-BERIT.
- **111:7** "His precepts" translates *piqqudav*.
- **111:9** "Redemption" translates *peduth*. "Holy and awesome is his name" translates *qadosh ve-nora shemo*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **111:10** "The fear of the LORD is the beginning of wisdom" translates *reshit chokhmah yirat YHWH*. The wording must be kept matched with Proverbs 1:7 and 9:10 and Job 28:28. "All who practise it" translates *kol-osehem*, "all who do them", the precepts.
- **112:1** "Blessed is the one who fears the LORD" translates *ashrei ish yare et-YHWH*.
- **112:2** "Mighty in the land" translates *gibbor ba-arets*.
- **112:3** "His righteousness endures for ever" translates *tsidqato omedet la-ad*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH. The same words are said of the LORD in 111:3.
- **112:4** "Light dawns in the darkness for the upright" translates *zarach ba-choshekh or la-yesharim*. "He is gracious, merciful and righteous" may refer to the upright man or to the LORD. The three words are used of the LORD in 111:3 and 4.
- **112:5** "Deals generously and lends" translates *chonen u-malveh*.
- **112:7** "Bad news" translates *shemu'ah ra'ah*.
- **112:8** "Until he looks in triumph on his adversaries" is as in 118:7.
- **112:9** "He has distributed freely" translates *pizzar*. The verse is quoted in 2 Corinthians 9:9. "His horn is exalted in honour" is a figure of strength.
- **113** Psalms 113 to 118 are the Hallel, the praise sung at the festivals. Psalm 113 has no heading in the Hebrew.
- **113:5** "Who is like the LORD our God?" is a form used in the old songs (see Exodus 15:11).
- **113:6** "Who looks far down on the heavens and the earth" translates *ha-mashpili lir'ot ba-shamayim u-va-arets*, "who stoops to see".
- **113:7** "The ash heap" translates *ashpot*. The verses 7 and 8 are like 1 Samuel 2:8.
- **113:9** "He gives the barren woman a home" translates *moshivi aqeret ha-bayit*, "he settles the barren woman of the house".
- **114** Psalm 114 has no heading in the Hebrew. In the Greek it is joined with Psalm 115 as one psalm.
- **114:1** "A people of strange language" translates *am lo'ez*.
- **114:2** "Judah became his sanctuary" translates *hayetah Yehudah le-qodsho*. See entry H-QADOSH. "His dominion" translates *mamshelotav*.
- **114:3** "The sea looked and fled. The Jordan turned back" refers to the sea at the exodus and the Jordan at the entry to the land.
- **114:7** "Tremble, earth, at the presence of the Lord" translates *mi-lifnei adon chuli arets*. The divine name is not used, so that the line holds the surprise until verse 8.
- **114:8** "Who turns the rock into a pool of water" refers to Exodus 17:6 and Numbers 20:11.
- **115** Psalm 115 has no heading in the Hebrew. In the Greek it is joined with Psalm 114.
- **115:1** "For the sake of your steadfast love and your faithfulness" translates *al-chasdekha al-amittekha*. See entry H-HESED.
- **115:2** "Where is their God?" is the mockery of the nations (see 42:3 and 79:10).
- **115:3** "He does all that he pleases" says that God is not bound by the idols' kind of weakness.
- **115:4** The verses 4 to 8 are nearly the same as 135:15-18. "The work of human hands" translates *ma'aseh yedei adam*.
- **115:8** "Become like them" translates *kemohem yihyu*, which says that those who make idols become dead like them.
- **115:9** "Israel", "the house of Aaron" and "you who fear the LORD" are three groups, in which the third may include those of other peoples who worship the LORD. "He is their help and their shield" translates *ezram u-maginnam hu*.
- **115:14** "May the LORD give you increase" translates *yosef YHWH aleikhem*.
- **115:16** "The earth he has given to humankind" recalls Genesis 1:28.
- **115:17** "The dead do not praise the LORD, nor do any who go down into silence" is rendered as written (see 6:5 and 88:10-12). "Silence" translates *dumah*, a name of Sheol.
- **116** Psalm 116 has no heading in the Hebrew. In the Greek it is divided in two, the first (114) ending at verse 9 and the second (115) beginning at verse 10.
- **116:1** "I love the LORD" translates *ahavti ki-yishma YHWH*, in which the Hebrew has no object, so that it reads "I love, because the LORD hears". The WABV text supplies "the LORD" as the natural object.
- **116:3** "The snares of death" translates *chevlei-mavet*, "the cords of death", as in 18:4. "Sheol" is the place of the dead. See entry H-SHEOL.
- **116:4** "Deliver my soul" translates *malletah nafshi*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **116:6** "The simple" translates *peta'im*. "When I was brought low" translates *dallothi*.
- **116:7** "Return, my soul, to your rest" translates *shuvi nafshi li-mnuchayikhi*. See entry H-SHUV. "Has dealt bountifully" translates *gamal*.
- **116:10** "I believed, even when I spoke" translates *he'emanti ki adabber*, which may be read "I believed, for I speak" or "I kept faith when I said". The Greek is quoted in 2 Corinthians 4:13 ("I believed, and so I spoke").
- **116:11** "In my alarm" translates *be-chofzi*. "All human beings are liars" is quoted in Romans 3:4.
- **116:12** "His benefits" translates *tagmulohi*.
- **116:13** "The cup of salvation" translates *kos yeshu'ot*, which may be a cup of thanks at the thank offering.
- **116:15** "Precious in the sight of the LORD is the death of his faithful ones" translates *yaqar be-einei YHWH ha-mmavetah la-chasidav*, in which *yaqar* may mean "precious" or "costly", and the line may say that the LORD does not give up the death of his faithful lightly. See entry H-HESED.
- **116:16** "The son of your maidservant" is as in 86:16. "You have loosed my bonds" translates *pitachta le-moserai*.
- **117** Psalm 117 is the shortest psalm and the shortest chapter in the Bible. It is quoted in Romans 15:11.
- **117:2** "Great is his steadfast love toward us" translates *gavar aleinu chasdo*. See entry H-HESED.
- **118** Psalm 118 has no heading in the Hebrew.
- **118:1** "His steadfast love endures for ever" is the refrain of verses 1 to 4 and 29. See entry H-HESED. The three groups are Israel, the house of Aaron and those who fear the LORD.
- **118:5** "Set me free" translates *ba-merchav*, "in a broad place" (see 18:19).
- **118:6** "The LORD is on my side" translates *YHWH li*. The verse is quoted in Hebrews 13:6.
- **118:10** "I cut them off" translates *amilam*, a rare verb that may mean "I cut them down". It is rendered as written.
- **118:12** "Like bees" and "like a fire among thorns" are figures of the speed and the end of the attack.
- **118:13** "I was pushed hard, so that I was falling" translates *dacho dechitani li-nfol*, in which the verb is second person ("you pushed me"), and some read it as "he pushed me".
- **118:14** "My strength and my song" translates *ozzi ve-zimrat Yah*, as in Exodus 15:2 and Isaiah 12:2.
- **118:18** "The LORD has disciplined me severely" translates *yassor yisserani Yah*.
- **118:19** "The gates of righteousness" translates *sha'arei-tsedeq*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **118:22** "The stone that the builders rejected has become the cornerstone" translates *even ma'asu ha-bonim hayetah le-rosh pinnah*, in which *rosh pinnah* may mean "the head of the corner", the corner foundation stone or the capstone. The verse is quoted in Matthew 21:42, Mark 12:10-11, Luke 20:17, Acts 4:11 and 1 Peter 2:7.
- **118:25** "Save us, we pray, LORD" translates *anna YHWH hoshi'ah na*, which gives the Greek and Aramaic word "Hosanna". "Give us success" translates *hatsli'chah na*.
- **118:26** "Blessed is the one who comes in the name of the LORD" is quoted in Matthew 21:9 and 23:39. "We bless you from the house of the LORD" is the blessing of the priests to the pilgrims.
- **118:27** "He has made his light shine on us" translates *va-ya'er lanu*. "Bind the festal sacrifice with cords, up to the horns of the altar" is hard in the Hebrew (*isru-chag ba-avotim ad qarnot ha-mizbeach*), in which *chag* may mean "the festival" or "the festal animal".
- **119** Psalm 119 has no heading in the Hebrew. The numbering is the same in Hebrew and English. In the Greek and Latin numbering it is 118. It is an acrostic of twenty-two stanzas of eight verses, in which every verse of a stanza begins with the same letter of the Hebrew alphabet. The WABV text does not try to reproduce this, and the stanza headings give the letters. The psalm uses eight words for the LORD's instruction, and the WABV text keeps one English word for each: "teaching" (*torah*), "testimonies" (*edot*), "precepts" (*piqqudim*), "statutes" (*chuqqim*), "commandments" (*mitsvot*), "rules" (*mishpatim*), "word" (*davar*) and "promise" (*imrah*). Where a verse has *imrah* in the plural (as in 119:103) the WABV text gives "promises". In 119:57 and 119:161 the plural of *davar* is "words".
- **119:1** "Blessed are those whose way is blameless" translates *ashrei temimei-derekh*. "The teaching of the LORD" translates *torat YHWH*. See entry H-TORAH. The same word is used for "teaching" throughout the psalm.
- **119:2** "His testimonies" translates *edotav*.
- **119:4** "Your precepts" translates *piqqudekha*. "Diligently" translates *me'od*.
- **119:5** "May my ways be steadfast" translates *yikkonu derakhai*.
- **119:7** "Your righteous rules" translates *mishpetei tsidqekha*.
- **119:8** "Do not utterly forsake me" translates *al-ta'azveni ad-me'od*.
- **119:9** "His way pure" translates *orcho*. "Your word" is *devarekha*.
- **119:11** "I have stored up your promise in my heart" translates *tsafanti imratekha*.
- **119:13** "The rules of your mouth" translates *mishpetei-fikha*.
- **119:15** "I will meditate" translates *asichah*.
- **119:17** "Deal bountifully" translates *gemol*.
- **119:18** "Wonderful things" translates *nifla'ot*.
- **119:19** "A stranger on the earth" translates *ger anokhi va-arets*.
- **119:20** "My soul is consumed with longing" translates *garesah nafshi le-ta'avah*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **119:21** "The insolent" translates *zedim*.
- **119:24** "My counsellors" translates *anshei atsati*, "the men of my counsel".
- **119:25** "My soul clings to the dust" is a figure of near death.
- **119:28** "Melts away" translates *datfah*.
- **119:29** "False ways" translates *derekh sheqer*.
- **119:30** "The way of faithfulness" translates *derekh emunah*.
- **119:32** "You enlarge my heart" translates *tarchiv libbi*.
- **119:33** "To the end" translates *eqev*, which may also mean "as a reward".
- **119:36** "Selfish gain" translates *betsa*.
- **119:37** "Worthless things" translates *shav*.
- **119:38** "That you may be feared" translates *asher le-yir'atekha*, which is hard and may be read "which is for those who fear you".
- **119:40** "In your righteousness" translates *be-tsidqatekha*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **119:41** "Your steadfast love" translates *chasdekha*. See entry H-HESED. "According to your promise" translates *ke-imratekha*.
- **119:45** "A wide place" translates *ba-rechavah*.
- **119:53** "Hot indignation" translates *zal'afah*.
- **119:54** "The house of my stay" translates *beit megurai*, the house of the poet's wandering.
- **119:57** "I promise to keep your words" translates *amarti lishmor devarekha*, literally "I have said that I will keep your words".
- **119:58** "I entreat your favour" translates *chillitti fanekha*.
- **119:61** "The cords of the wicked" translates *chevlei resha'im*.
- **119:66** "Good judgement" translates *ta'am tov*, which may also mean "good taste".
- **119:67** "Went astray" translates *shogeg*. "I keep your promise" translates *imratekha shamarti*.
- **119:69** "Smear me with lies" translates *tafelu alai sheqer*.
- **119:70** "Unfeeling like fat" translates *tafash ka-chelev libbam*.
- **119:75** "In faithfulness you have afflicted me" says that the affliction is not a sign that God has been unfaithful.
- **119:78** "They have wronged me with falsehood" translates *sheqer ivvetuni*.
- **119:80** "Blameless in your statutes" translates *tamim be-chuqqekha*.
- **119:81** "My soul longs for your salvation" translates *kaltah le-tshu'atekha nafshi*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **119:83** "Like a wineskin in the smoke" translates *ke-nod be-qitor*, a skin that is dried and shrivelled.
- **119:85** "Pitfalls" translates *shichot*.
- **119:89** "Your word is firmly fixed in the heavens" translates *devarkha nitsav ba-shamayim*.
- **119:96** "A limit to all perfection" translates *lekhol-tikhlah ra'iti qets*, which may also be read "an end to all that is complete".
- **119:97** "My meditation" translates *sichati*.
- **119:103** "How sweet are your promises to my taste" translates *mah-nimletsu le-chikki imratekha*.
- **119:105** "A lamp" is *ner* and "a light" is *or*.
- **119:108** "My freewill offerings of praise" translates *nidvot fi*, "the freewill offerings of my mouth".
- **119:109** "I hold my life in my hand" translates *nafshi be-khappi*, which may also mean "my life is in danger".
- **119:111** "My heritage" translates *nachalti*.
- **119:113** "The double-minded" translates *se'afim*, a rare word.
- **119:114** "My hiding place" translates *sitri*.
- **119:117** "Have regard" translates *esh'ah*.
- **119:118** "You spurn" translates *salita*.
- **119:119** "Dross" translates *sigim*.
- **119:120** "Your judgements" translates *mishpatekha*.
- **119:121** "What is just and right" translates *mishpat va-tsedeq*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **119:122** "Give your servant a pledge of good" translates *arov avdekha le-tov*, in which *arov* means "be surety for".
- **119:123** "Your righteous promise" translates *imrat tsidqekha*.
- **119:126** "It is time for the LORD to act, for your teaching has been broken" translates *et la-asot la-YHWH heferu toratekha*. It is rendered as written.
- **119:128** "I consider all your precepts to be right" translates *kol-piqqudei khol yishsharti*, which is hard and may be read "I hold all the precepts about everything to be right".
- **119:129** "Wonderful" translates *peli'ot*.
- **119:130** "The unfolding of your words gives light" translates *petach devarekha ya'ir*, in which *petach* may mean "opening" or "entrance". "The simple" translates *peta'im*.
- **119:132** "As is your way with those who love your name" translates *ke-mishpat le-ohavei shemekha*, in which *mishpat* means "custom".
- **119:136** "My eyes shed streams of tears" translates *palgei-mayim yardu einai*.
- **119:138** "In righteousness" translates *tsedeq*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **119:139** "My zeal consumes me" translates *tsimmetatni qin'ati*.
- **119:140** "Well tried" translates *tseruphah*, "refined".
- **119:145** "With my whole heart I cry" translates *qarati be-khol-lev*.
- **119:147** "I rise before dawn" translates *qiddamti ba-neshef*.
- **119:149** "According to your justice" translates *ke-mishpatekha*.
- **119:150** "Evil purpose" translates *zimmah*.
- **119:152** "Long have I known" translates *qedem yada'ti*, "from of old I have known".
- **119:154** "Plead my cause and redeem me" translates *rivah rivi u-ge'alleni*. The verb *ga'al* is the verb of the redeemer (see entry H-GOEL).
- **119:158** "The faithless" translates *bogedim*. "They do not keep your promise" translates *asher imratekha lo shamaru*.
- **119:160** "The sum of your word" translates *rosh-devarkha*, in which *rosh* means "head" or "sum" and may also be read "the beginning of your word".
- **119:162** "Great spoil" translates *shalal rav*.
- **119:164** "Seven times a day" is a figure of full and constant praise.
- **119:165** "Great peace" translates *shalom rav*. See entry H-SHALOM. "Nothing can make them stumble" translates *ve-ein-lamo mikhshol*.
- **119:169** "My cry" translates *rinnati*, and "my plea" in verse 170 is *techinnati*.
- **119:171** "My lips will pour forth praise" translates *tabba'nah sefatai tehillah*.
- **119:172** "My tongue will sing of your promise" translates *ta'an leshoni imratekha*.
- **119:175** "Let your rules help me" translates *u-mishpatekha ya'azruni*.
- **119:176** "I have gone astray like a lost sheep. Seek your servant, for I do not forget your commandments" ends the psalm with the poet's confession and his prayer to be found.
- **120** Psalms 120 to 134 are the Songs of Ascents (*shir ha-ma'alot*). The word *ma'alot* may mean "ascents" (the going up of pilgrims to Jerusalem), "steps" (the steps of the temple) or the "step-like" repetition of words from line to line in many of these psalms. In all of them the heading is part of verse 1 in the Hebrew, so that the numbering is the same in Hebrew and English. In the Greek and Latin numbering they are one lower, from 119 to 133.
- **120:2** "Lying lips" translates *sifat-sheqer*, and "a deceitful tongue" translates *lashon remiyyah*.
- **120:3** "What will be given to you, and what more will be done to you" translates *mah-yittein lekha u-mah-yosif lakh*, which is the form of an oath of cursing ("so may God do to you, and more").
- **120:4** "A warrior's sharp arrows, with glowing coals of the broom tree" is the answer to verse 3. The coals of the broom tree (*gachalei retamim*) burn for a long time.
- **120:5** "Meshech" is far to the north and "Kedar" is in the desert to the south-east. The poet speaks of living among people far from his own.
- **120:6** "Those who hate peace" translates *soneh shalom*. See entry H-SHALOM.
- **120:7** "I am for peace" translates *ani shalom*, literally "I am peace".
- **121:1** "I lift up my eyes to the hills" refers to the hills of Jerusalem, or to the high places of the other gods. "From where does my help come?" is a question and not a doubt. The Hebrew has *me-ayin yavo ezri*.
- **121:2** "My help comes from the LORD" translates *ezri me-im YHWH*.
- **121:3** "He will not let your foot slip" translates *al-yiten la-mot raglekha*. "He who keeps you" translates *shomerekha*. The verb *shamar* ("keep") is repeated six times in the psalm (verses 3, 4, 5, 7 twice and 8), and the WABV text keeps one English word for it.
- **121:4** "Will neither slumber nor sleep" is the answer to the prayer in 44:23.
- **121:5** "Your shade at your right hand" translates *tsillekha al-yad yeminekha*.
- **121:6** "The sun will not strike you by day, nor the moon by night" refers to heatstroke and to the harm that was thought to come from the moon.
- **121:7** "Your life" translates *nafshekha*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **121:8** "Your going out and your coming in" is the whole of a person's life and work.
- **122:1** "I was glad when they said to me, 'Let us go to the house of the LORD'" is the pilgrim's joy.
- **122:3** "Built as a city that is bound firmly together" translates *shechubbrah-lah yachdav*, which may mean "compact" or "joined together", or may refer to the unity of the city.
- **122:4** "As was decreed for Israel" translates *edut le-Yisra'el*, literally "a testimony for Israel". It refers to the law of the three yearly feasts (Deuteronomy 16:16).
- **122:6** "Pray for the peace of Jerusalem" translates *sha'alu shelom Yerushalaim*, which has a play on the sounds of *shalom* and *Yerushalaim*. See entry H-SHALOM. "May those who love you be secure" translates *yishlayu ohavayikh*.
- **122:9** "I will seek your good" says that the poet will work for the welfare of the city.
- **123:2** "As the eyes of servants look to the hand of their master" is a figure of waiting for a sign.
- **123:3** "Contempt" translates *buz*.
- **123:4** "Of those who are at ease" translates *ha-sha'ananim*. "Of the proud" translates *li-ge'ei yonim*, which is hard and may be read "of the proud oppressors".
- **124:1** "If it had not been the LORD who was on our side" translates *lulei YHWH she-hayah lanu*.
- **124:5** "The raging waters" translates *ha-mayim ha-zeidonim*.
- **124:7** "We have escaped like a bird from the snare of the fowlers" is a figure of rescue.
- **124:8** "Our help is in the name of the LORD, who made heaven and earth" is as in 121:2.
- **125:1** "Those who trust in the LORD are like Mount Zion" is a figure of the security of those who trust.
- **125:3** "The sceptre of wickedness will not rest on the land allotted to the righteous" refers to foreign rule over the land. "Lest the righteous stretch out their hands to do wrong" gives the reason.
- **125:5** "The LORD will lead away with evildoers" is rendered as written. "Crooked ways" translates *aqalqalotam*. "Peace be on Israel" translates *shalom al-Yisra'el*. See entry H-SHALOM.
- **126:1** "When the LORD restored the fortunes of Zion" translates *be-shuv YHWH et-shivat Tsiyyon*, in which the phrase may also mean "when the LORD brought back the returning of Zion". "We were like those who dream" translates *hayinu ke-cholemim*, which may also be read "like those who were healed".
- **126:2** "Then they said among the nations" shows that the nations saw what God had done.
- **126:3** "The LORD has done great things for us" may be in the past or in the present.
- **126:4** "Restore our fortunes" translates *shuvah YHWH et-shevitenu*. "Like streams in the Negeb" translates *ka-afiqim ba-negev*, the dry riverbeds of the south that fill with water after rain.
- **126:5** "Those who sow in tears will reap with shouts of joy" is a saying of hope.
- **127** "Of Solomon" translates *li-Shelomoh* (see Psalm 72). The psalm plays on the words *bayit* ("house") and *banim* ("sons").
- **127:1** "The house" may be a home, the temple or a dynasty. "The watchman stays awake in vain" says that human watching is not enough.
- **127:2** "He gives sleep to his beloved" translates *ken yitten li-ydido shenah*, which may also be read "he gives to his beloved in sleep" or "so he gives his beloved sleep".
- **127:3** "A heritage" translates *nachalah* and "a reward" is *sachar*.
- **127:5** "When he speaks with his enemies in the gate" refers to the court at the city gate, where a man with many sons was strong.
- **128:1** "Blessed is everyone who fears the LORD" translates *ashrei kol-yare YHWH*.
- **128:3** "Your wife will be like a fruitful vine within your house. Your children will be like olive shoots around your table" is the picture of a blessed household.
- **128:5** "May the LORD bless you from Zion" is the blessing of the priest.
- **128:6** "Peace be on Israel" translates *shalom al-Yisra'el*. See entry H-SHALOM.
- **129:1** "They have afflicted me from my youth" refers to Israel's long suffering, from Egypt.
- **129:3** "The ploughers ploughed on my back. They made long their furrows" translates *al-gabbi charshu charshim he'erikhu le-ma'anitam*, a figure of scars.
- **129:4** "The LORD is righteous" translates *YHWH tsaddiq*. "He has cut the cords of the wicked" says that Israel is freed from the yoke.
- **129:5** "May all who hate Zion be put to shame and turned back" is rendered as written. The verses 5 to 8 are a wish against Israel's enemies. "Like the grass on the housetops" is a figure of what withers.
- **129:8** "The blessing of the LORD be on you" is the greeting that is not given to them.
- **130** Psalm 130 is one of the seven penitential psalms of later tradition.
- **130:1** "Out of the depths" translates *mi-ma'amaqqim*, the deep waters.
- **130:3** "If you, LORD, should mark iniquities" translates *im-avonot tishmor-Yah*, "if you keep a record of iniquities".
- **130:4** "But with you there is forgiveness, that you may be feared" translates *ki-immekha ha-selichah le-ma'an tivva'*. The word *selichah* is used only of God's forgiveness.
- **130:5** "My soul waits" translates *qiv'ah nafshi*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **130:6** "More than watchmen for the morning" is repeated, as a figure of waiting.
- **130:7** "Steadfast love" is *chesed*. See entry H-HESED. "Plentiful redemption" translates *harbeh immo fedut*.
- **130:8** "He will redeem Israel from all his iniquities" translates *hu yifdeh et-Yisra'el mi-kol avonotav*, in which the verb *padah* means "ransom" (see 49:7 and 49:15).
- **131:1** "Things too great and too wonderful for me" translates *gedolot u-nifla'ot mimmenni*, as in Job 42:3.
- **131:2** "I have calmed and quieted my soul" translates *shivviti ve-domamti nafshi*. "Like a weaned child with its mother" translates *ke-gamul alei immo*, in which *alei* may mean "with" or "upon".
- **132:1** "All the hardships he endured" translates *et kol-unnoto*, which may also be read "all his humility" or "all his devotion".
- **132:2** "The Mighty One of Jacob" translates *avir Ya'aqov*, an old title (see Genesis 49:24). The vow of David is not told in the books of Samuel.
- **132:6** "Ephrathah" is Bethlehem or the land of Ephraim. "The fields of Jaar" refers to Kiriath-jearim, where the ark stayed (see 1 Samuel 7:1-2).
- **132:7** "His footstool" refers to the ark (see 99:5).
- **132:8** The verses 8 to 10 are nearly the same as 2 Chronicles 6:41-42, and the wording must be kept matched where the Hebrew is the same. "The ark of your might" translates *aron uzzekha*.
