- **132:9** "Righteousness" translates *tsedeq*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH. "Your faithful ones" translates *chasidekha*. See entry H-HESED.
- **132:10** "Your anointed one" translates *meshichekha*. See entry H-MASHIACH.
- **132:12** "If your sons keep my covenant" is conditional. See entry H-BERIT. The promise in 2 Samuel 7:14-16 is not conditional in the same way.
- **132:15** "Her provisions" translates *tsedah*.
- **132:17** "A horn sprout for David" and "a lamp for my anointed" are figures of strength and of the continuing line.
- **133:1** "When brothers live together in unity" translates *shevet achim gam-yachad*.
- **133:2** "The collar of his robes" translates *pi middotav*, "the opening of his garment".
- **133:3** "The dew of Hermon, which falls on the mountains of Zion" is a figure, since Hermon is far to the north. "The LORD has commanded the blessing" translates *tsivvah YHWH et-ha-berakhah*.
- **134:1** "All you servants of the LORD, who stand by night in the house of the LORD" refers to the priests and Levites on night duty.
- **134:2** "Lift up your hands to the holy place" translates *se'u yedekhem qodesh*, which may mean "in holiness" or "toward the sanctuary". See entry H-QADOSH.
- **134:3** "May the LORD bless you from Zion, he who made heaven and earth" is the closing blessing, spoken in answer to the call of verses 1 and 2.
- **135** In Psalm 140 the Hebrew heading is verse 1, so that English 140:1-13 is Hebrew 140:2-14. In Psalm 142 the Hebrew heading is verse 1, so that English 142:1-7 is Hebrew 142:2-8. Psalms 135 to 139, 141 and 143 to 145 are numbered alike in Hebrew and English. In the Greek and Latin numbering these psalms are one lower, from 134 to 144. Psalm 135 has no heading in the Hebrew.
- **135:2** "Who stand in the house of the LORD" refers to the priests and Levites.
- **135:4** "His own possession" translates *segullah*, a treasured possession (see Exodus 19:5).
- **135:5** "Our Lord is above all gods" translates *Adoneinu mi-kol-elohim*.
- **135:7** "He makes lightnings for the rain and brings out the wind from his storehouses" is nearly the same as Jeremiah 10:13 and 51:16.
- **135:10** The verses 10 to 12 refer to the conquest of the land. "Sihon" and "Og" are the kings of Numbers 21.
- **135:14** "Will vindicate his people and have compassion on his servants" is nearly the same as Deuteronomy 32:36.
- **135:15** The verses 15 to 18 are nearly the same as 115:4-8, and the wording is kept matched where the Hebrew is the same. The Hebrew of 135 does not have the lines about the nose, the hands and the feet that are in 115:6-7.
- **135:17** "Nor is there any breath in their mouths" translates *af ein-yesh-ruach be-fihem*. See entry H-RUACH.
- **135:19** "House of Levi" is added here to the three groups of 115:9-11 (Israel, the house of Aaron and those who fear the LORD).
- **136** Psalm 136 has no heading in the Hebrew. It is called the Great Hallel in Jewish tradition. The refrain "for his steadfast love endures for ever" (*ki le-olam chasdo*) is in each of the twenty-six verses. See entry H-HESED.
- **136:2** "The God of gods" and "the Lord of lords" are as in Deuteronomy 10:17.
- **136:5** "By understanding made the heavens" translates *le-oseh ha-shamayim bi-tevunah*, as in Proverbs 3:19.
- **136:6** "Spread out the earth above the waters" refers to Genesis 1:9-10.
- **136:8** "To rule over the day" translates *le-memshelet ba-yom*.
- **136:13** "The Red Sea" translates *yam suf*, "the Sea of Reeds".
- **136:15** "Overthrew" translates *ni'er*, "shook off".
- **136:23** "In our low estate" translates *be-shiflenu*.
- **136:25** "All flesh" translates *kol-basar*.
- **136:26** "The God of heaven" is a title that is used mostly in the later books (see Ezra 1:2 and Nehemiah 1:4).
- **137** Psalm 137 has no heading in the Hebrew. The Greek adds "of David, by Jeremiah".
- **137:1** "The rivers of Babylon" translates *naharot Bavel*, the canals and rivers of Babylonia.
- **137:2** "The willows" translates *aravim*, and "our lyres" is *kinnorotenu*.
- **137:3** "Our tormentors" translates the rare word *tolaleinu*, which is hard and may mean "those who plunder us".
- **137:5** "Let my right hand forget its skill" translates *tishkach yemini*, in which the verb *shakach* means "forget". Some read it as "wither".
- **137:7** "Remember, LORD, against the Edomites the day of Jerusalem" refers to the part of Edom in the fall of Jerusalem (see Obadiah 10-14, Ezekiel 25:12 and Lamentations 4:21). "Lay it bare" translates *aru*.
- **137:8** "Daughter of Babylon" is a figure of the city. "Doomed to be destroyed" translates *ha-shedudah*, which may mean "devastated" or "devastator".
- **137:9** "Blessed will be the one who takes your little ones and dashes them against the rock" is rendered as written, with no softening. It refers to the horrors of the wars of the time (see Isaiah 13:16, Hosea 13:16 and Nahum 3:10).
- **138** The Greek adds the names of Haggai and Zechariah to the heading.
- **138:1** "Before the gods" translates *neged elohim*, which may mean the gods of the nations, the heavenly beings or judges.
- **138:2** "Your holy temple" translates *heikhal qodshekha*. See entry H-QADOSH. "Your steadfast love" is *chasdekha*. See entry H-HESED. "For you have exalted above all things your name and your word" translates *ki-higdalta al-kol-shimkha imratekha*, which is hard and may be read "you have exalted your word above all your name".
- **138:3** "You made me bold, with strength in my soul" translates *tarhiveni be-nafshi oz*. See entry H-NEPHESH. The verb *rahav* is rare and may mean "increase" or "embolden".
- **138:6** "Though the LORD is high, he regards the lowly" is as in 113:5-6.
- **138:7** "You preserve my life" translates *techayyeni*.
- **138:8** "Will fulfil his purpose for me" translates *yigmor ba'adi*, "will complete for me".
- **139:1** "You have searched me and known me" translates *chaqartani va-tedda*. The psalm has no wish to hide from God, and it ends with a prayer to be searched.
- **139:2** "You discern my thoughts from afar" translates *bavta le-re'i me-rachoq*.
- **139:3** "You search out my path and my lying down" translates *orchi ve-rivi zeritah*, in which the verb *zarah* means "winnow" and may be read "you sift". "Are acquainted with" translates *hiskanta*.
- **139:5** "You hem me in" translates *tsartani*, "you besiege me" or "you enclose me". "And lay your hand on me" may be a figure of protection or of pressure.
- **139:6** "Too wonderful" translates *peliyyah* and "it is high" translates *nisgevah*.
- **139:7** "Your Spirit" translates *ruachekha*. See entry H-RUACH. The word is parallel to "your presence" (*panekha*), and it may also be read "your breath".
- **139:8** "Sheol" is the place of the dead. See entry H-SHEOL. "If I make my bed in Sheol" translates *va-atsia Sheol*, in which the verb is rare.
- **139:9** "The wings of the morning" translates *kanfei shachar*, a figure of swift flight toward the east. "The farthest parts of the sea" translates *acharit yam*, the far west.
- **139:13** "My inward parts" translates *kilyotai*, "my kidneys". "You knitted me together" translates *tesukkeni*, "you wove me".
- **139:14** "I am fearfully and wonderfully made" translates *nora'ot nifleti*, which is hard and may be read "I am distinct in awesome ways". The Greek has "you have made me wonderful". "My soul knows it very well" uses *nafshi*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **139:15** "My frame" translates *otsmi*. "Woven in the depths of the earth" is a figure of the womb (see Job 10:8-11).
- **139:16** "My unformed substance" translates *golmi*, a rare word that may mean "my embryo". "The days that were formed for me" translates *yamim yuttsaru*. "When as yet there was none of them" translates *ve-lo echad bahem*, which is hard and may be read "and not one of them was yet". The book that is mentioned is as in Exodus 32:32-33 and Psalm 69:28.
- **139:17** "Your thoughts" translates *re'ekha*.
- **139:19** "Oh that you would kill the wicked, God" translates *im-tiqtol Eloah rasha*, "if only you would kill the wicked". It is rendered as written. "Bloodthirsty people" translates *anshei damim*.
- **139:20** "They speak against you with malicious intent" translates *asher yomerukha li-mzimmah*, which is hard. "Your enemies take your name in vain" translates *nasu la-shav areka*, in which *areka* is unclear and may be read "your cities".
- **139:22** "I hate them with complete hatred" translates *taklit sin'ah sena'tim*, and it is rendered as written, with no softening.
- **139:24** "Any hurtful way" translates *derekh otsev*, which may mean "a way of idols" or "a way of pain". "The way everlasting" translates *derekh olam*, which may also be read "the ancient way".
- **140** "Deliver me, LORD, from evil people" begins the prayer against the violent. The speaker's enemies are shown as hunters and as serpents.
- **140:3** "The venom of vipers" translates *chamat akhshuv*. It is quoted in Romans 3:13.
- **140:7** "You have covered my head in the day of battle" translates *sakkota le-roshi be-yom navshek*, a figure of protection in battle.
- **140:8** "Do not further their evil plot" translates *zemamo al-tafeq*, which is hard and may be read "do not let their plan go forth".
- **140:9** "As for the head of those who surround me" translates *rosh mesibbai*, which is hard, and some read it "those who surround me raise their heads". "The mischief of their lips" translates *amal sifteihem*.
- **140:10** "Let burning coals fall on them" follows the reading tradition (*yamitu*). "Miry pits" translates *mahamorot*, a rare word. The prayer is rendered as written.
- **140:11** "The slanderer" translates *ish lashon*, "a man of tongue".
- **140:12** "Will maintain the cause of the afflicted" translates *yaasheh YHWH din ani*.
- **141:2** "Incense" translates *qetoret* and "the evening sacrifice" translates *minchat-arev*.
- **141:3** "Set a guard, LORD, over my mouth" translates *shitah YHWH shomrah le-fi*. "The door of my lips" translates *dal sefatai*.
- **141:4** "Delicacies" translates *man'ammeihem*, a rare word.
- **141:5** The verse is very hard in the Hebrew. "Let a righteous person strike me. It is a kindness" translates *yehelmeni tsaddiq chesed*, and "it is oil for my head" translates *shemen rosh*, and "yet my prayer is continually against their evil deeds" translates *ki-od ve-tefillati be-ra'otehem*, which may be read "for my prayer is still against their evil" or "my prayer is still for them in their evil". The line is rendered with no softening.
- **141:6** "When their judges are thrown over the cliff" translates *nishmetu be-yedei-sela shofeteihem*, which is hard and may be read "their judges are thrown down on the sides of the rock".
- **141:7** "As when one ploughs and breaks up the earth, so our bones are scattered at the mouth of Sheol" is hard in the Hebrew (*ke-focheah u-voqe'a ba-arets*). See entry H-SHEOL.
- **141:8** "Do not leave me defenceless" translates *al-te'ar nafshi*, "do not pour out my soul".
- **141:10** "While I pass by safely" says that the poet is spared.
- **142** "A maskil" is explained at Psalm 32. The cave may be the cave of Adullam or of En-gedi (see 1 Samuel 22:1 and 24:3). "A prayer" translates *tefillah* (see Psalm 17).
- **142:2** "My complaint" translates *sichi* and "my trouble" translates *tsarati*.
- **142:3** "My spirit faints within me" translates *be-hit'atef alai ruchi*. See entry H-RUACH. "You know my way" is the poet's comfort.
- **142:4** "Look to the right and see" refers to the place of the defender (see 109:31 and 121:5). "There is no one who takes notice of me" translates *ein-makkir li*. "No one cares for my soul" translates *ein doresh le-nafshi*, "no one seeks for my soul". See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **142:7** "Bring my soul out of prison" translates *hotsi'ah mi-masger nafshi*, in which *masger* is a place of confinement, whether the cave or a figure. "The righteous will surround me" translates *bi yakhtiru tsaddiqim*, in which the verb is rare and may mean "crown me" or "gather round me".
- **143:1** "In your faithfulness answer me, in your righteousness" translates *be-emunatekha aneni be-tsidqatekha*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **143:2** "For no one living is righteous before you" translates *ki lo-yitsdaq lefanekha kol-chai*. It is rendered as written (see Romans 3:20 and Galatians 2:16).
- **143:3** "Like those long dead" translates *ke-metei olam*.
- **143:4** "Appalled" translates *yishtomem*, "stunned".
- **143:6** "I stretch out my hands to you" is a gesture of prayer.
- **143:8** "Your steadfast love" is *chasdekha*. See entry H-HESED.
- **143:10** "Your good Spirit" translates *ruchakha tovah*. See entry H-RUACH. The same phrase is used in Nehemiah 9:20. "On level ground" translates *be-erets mishor*.
- **143:12** "You will cut off my enemies and destroy all the adversaries of my soul" is rendered as written.
- **144** The verses 1 to 11 are close to Psalm 18 in several lines (18:2, 34, 47-48). The verses 3 and 4 are a form of 8:4 and Job 7:17.
- **144:1** "Who trains my hands for war" is as in 18:34.
- **144:2** "My steadfast love" translates *chasdi*, which is rare as a title of God. See entry H-HESED. "Who subdues peoples under me" follows the reading *ammim* in many Hebrew manuscripts and in the parallel in 18:47. The Hebrew text most often read has *ammi*, "my people". The WABV text departs from that reading and is flagged for the scholar.
- **144:4** "A breath" translates *hevel*.
- **144:5** "Bow your heavens, LORD, and come down. Touch the mountains so that they smoke" is as in 18:9 and 104:32.
- **144:7** "The many waters" are the powers of chaos. "Foreigners" translates *benei nekhar*, "sons of a foreign land".
- **144:8** "A right hand of falsehood" refers to the right hand raised in an oath that is false.
- **144:9** "A new song" is as in 33:3. "A ten-stringed harp" translates *nevel asor*.
- **144:12** The verses 12 to 15 are hard. The relative word *asher* may join them to the "foreigners" of verse 11 ("whose sons are like plants ..."), so that the verses describe the prosperity of the foreigners. The WABV text reads them as the prayer of the people, as the verse 15 suggests ("blessed are the people whose God is the LORD"). "Our daughters like corner pillars cut for the structure of a palace" translates *benoteinu ke-zaviyyot mechuttavot tavnit heikhal*, which is hard.
- **144:13** "Granaries" translates *mezavim*.
- **144:14** "Heavy with young" translates *mesubbalim*, a rare word. "Suffering no mishap or failure in bearing" translates *ein pherets ve-ein yotsett*, which is hard. "No cry of distress" translates *ve-ein tsevachah*.
- **145** "A song of praise" translates *tehillah*, the only psalm with this word in its heading, and the root of the Hebrew name of the whole book (*Tehillim*). Psalm 145 is an acrostic, in which each verse begins with a letter of the Hebrew alphabet in turn. The letter *nun* is missing in the Hebrew text most often read. The WABV text does not try to reproduce the acrostic.
- **145:1** "I will extol you, my God and King" translates *aromimkha Elohai ha-melekh*.
- **145:3** "His greatness is unsearchable" translates *ve-ligdulato ein cheqer*.
- **145:5** "On the glorious splendour of your majesty and on your wonderful works I will meditate" translates *hadar kevod hodekha ve-divrei nifle'otekha asichah*. The text is hard and the Greek and Syriac differ.
- **145:7** "Your righteousness" translates *tsidqatekha*. See entry H-TSEDAQAH.
- **145:8** "The LORD is gracious and merciful, slow to anger and abounding in steadfast love" repeats Exodus 34:6, and the wording is kept matched with the WABV text of that verse. See entry H-HESED.
- **145:10** "Your faithful ones" translates *chasidekha*. See entry H-HESED.
- **145:13** "The LORD is faithful in all his words and kind in all his works" is the line for the letter *nun*. It is in a Dead Sea scroll, in the Greek and in the Syriac, and in some Hebrew manuscripts, but it is not in the Hebrew text most often read. The WABV text gives it and marks it as a departure from that text. "Kind" translates *chasid*. See entry H-HESED.
- **145:14** "Upholds all who are falling" translates *somekh YHWH le-khol-ha-nofelim*.
- **145:16** "You open your hand" is as in 104:28. "Desire" translates *ratson*.
- **145:17** "Kind in all his works" translates *chasid be-khol-ma'asav*. See entry H-HESED.
- **145:20** "But all the wicked he will destroy" is rendered as written.
- **145:21** "All flesh" translates *kol-basar*. "His holy name" translates *shem qodsho*. See entry H-QADOSH.
- **146** Psalms 146 to 150 are the closing Hallelujah psalms of the Psalter. None has a heading in the Hebrew, and each begins and ends with "Praise the LORD" (*halelu-Yah*). They are numbered alike in Hebrew and English. In the Greek and Latin numbering Psalm 146 is 145, Psalm 147 is divided into two (146 for verses 1 to 11, and 147 for verses 12 to 20), and Psalms 148 to 150 are numbered alike. The Greek adds the names of Haggai and Zechariah to the headings of Psalms 146 to 148.
- **146:1** "My soul" translates *nafshi*. See entry H-NEPHESH.
- **146:3** "Princes" translates *nedivim*. "A human being" translates *ben adam*, "a son of man". "In whom there is no salvation" translates *she-ein lo teshu'ah*.
- **146:4** "His breath" translates *ruchu*. See entry H-RUACH. The word may also be read "his spirit". "His plans" translates *eshtonotav*, a rare word.
- **146:6** "Who keeps faith for ever" translates *ha-shomer emet le-olam*.
- **146:7** The verses 7 to 9 give the works of the LORD in six statements: justice for the oppressed, food for the hungry, freedom for the prisoners, sight for the blind, a lifting of the bowed down, and the care of the stranger, the widow and the fatherless.
- **146:9** "He brings to ruin" translates *ye'avvet*, "he makes crooked".
- **146:10** "The LORD will reign for ever" translates *yimlokh YHWH le-olam*.
- **147** The verses 1 to 11 and the verses 12 to 20 are two psalms in the Greek.
- **147:2** "The LORD builds up Jerusalem. He gathers the outcasts of Israel" refers to the rebuilding of the city after the exile (see Nehemiah 12:27-43).
- **147:3** "He heals the brokenhearted" is as in Isaiah 61:1.
- **147:4** "He determines the number of the stars. He gives to all of them their names" is as in Isaiah 40:26.
- **147:9** "The young ravens that cry" recalls Job 38:41.
- **147:10** "The legs of a man" is a figure of a runner or a soldier.
- **147:11** "His steadfast love" translates *chasdo*. See entry H-HESED.
- **147:14** "Peace" translates *shalom*. See entry H-SHALOM. "The finest of the wheat" translates *chelev chittim*, "the fat of wheat", as in 81:16.
- **147:15** "His word runs swiftly" translates *ad-meherah yarutz devaro*.
- **147:17** "His ice" translates *qarcho*, which is hard and may mean "his frost" or "his hail".
- **147:19** "His word", "his statutes" and "his rules" translate *devarav*, *chuqqav* and *mishpatav*, the same words as in Psalm 119.
- **147:20** "They do not know his rules" translates *u-mishpatim bal-yeda'um*, "and rules, they do not know them". It is rendered as written.
- **148:2** "His angels" translates *mal'akhav* and "his hosts" translates *tseva'av*.
- **148:4** "The waters above the heavens" refers to Genesis 1:7.
- **148:5** "For he commanded and they were created" is as in 33:9.
- **148:6** "He gave a decree, and it will not pass away" translates *choq-natan ve-lo ya'avor*, which may also be read "he gave a decree, and they will not transgress it".
- **148:7** "You great sea creatures" translates *tanninim*.
- **148:8** "Stormy wind fulfilling his word" translates *ruach se'arah oseh devaro*.
- **148:12** "Young men and young women together, old and young alike" translates *bachurim ve-gam-betulot zeqenim im-ne'arim*.
- **148:14** "He has raised up a horn for his people" is a figure of strength. "His faithful ones" translates *chasidav*. See entry H-HESED. "Who are near to him" translates *am qerovo*.
- **149:1** "A new song" is as in 96:1 and 98:1. "The faithful" translates *chasidim*. See entry H-HESED. The word is also used in verses 5 and 9 for "the faithful" and "his faithful ones".
- **149:2** "In his Maker" translates *be-osav*, a plural form.
- **149:4** "He adorns the humble with salvation" translates *yefa'er anavim bi-yeshu'ah*.
- **149:6** "The high praises of God" translates *romemot El*, and "two-edged swords" translates *cherev pifiyyot*.
- **149:7** "To execute vengeance on the nations" is rendered as written, with no softening and no comment. The verses 6 to 9 are among the hardest lines in the book.
- **149:9** "The judgement written" translates *mishpat katuv*, in which the writing is not named. It may refer to the law, to a decree, or to a writing of judgement in heaven.
- **150:1** "In his sanctuary" translates *be-qodsho*, which may also be read "in his holiness". See entry H-QADOSH. "His mighty heavens" translates *raqia uzzo*, "the firmament of his strength".
- **150:2** "His mighty deeds" translates *bi-gevurotav* and "his excellent greatness" translates *ke-rov gudlo*.
- **150:3** "Trumpet sound" translates *teqa shofar*, "the blast of the ram's horn". "Lute" is *nevel* and "harp" is *kinnor*.
- **150:4** "Tambourine" translates *tof*, "dance" is *machol*, "strings" translates *minnim* and "pipe" is *ugav*.
- **150:5** "Sounding cymbals" and "loud clashing cymbals" translate *tseltselei-shama* and *tseltselei teru'ah*.
- **150:6** "Everything that has breath" translates *kol ha-neshamah*. The word *neshamah* is the "breath of life" of Genesis 2:7, and it is a different word from *ruach*. The last words, "Praise the LORD", close the whole Psalter.

## Appendix B: Centre References

*Centre references sit here, not in the reading text. They are listed by psalm and verse.*

| Verse | Reference | Type |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1:1-6 | Jeremiah 17:5-8; Joshua 1:8; Proverbs 4:14-19; Matthew 7:24-27; Psalm 119:1-3 | Parallel: the two ways and the tree |
| 2:1-12 | Acts 4:25-28; 13:33; Hebrews 1:5; 5:5; Revelation 2:26-27; 12:5; 19:15; 2 Samuel 7:12-16; Psalm 110:1 | Parallel and allusion: the anointed king and the nations |
| 3:1-8 | 2 Samuel 15:13-17:29; Psalm 4:8; 27:1-3; 91:5-6; Proverbs 3:24 | Parallel: David and the night of trust |
| 4:1-8 | Ephesians 4:26; Numbers 6:24-26; Psalm 3:5; 63:6; 91:1-2; Isaiah 26:3 | Parallel: evening prayer and peace |
| 5:1-12 | Psalm 27:4; 30:5; 143:8; Romans 3:13; Proverbs 15:8; Habakkuk 1:13 | Parallel: morning prayer and the righteous God |
| 6:1-10 | Psalm 38:1-8; 88:10-12; 30:9; Isaiah 38:18; Matthew 7:23; John 12:27 | Parallel: prayer in sickness and the silence of Sheol |
| 7:1-17 | 1 Samuel 24:11-15; 26:23; Job 31:5-8; Psalm 9:15-16; 35:7-8; Proverbs 26:27 | Parallel: the oath of innocence and the judge |
| 8:1-9 | Genesis 1:26-28; Matthew 21:16; 1 Corinthians 15:27; Hebrews 2:5-9; Job 7:17-18; Psalm 144:3-4 | Parallel and allusion: the name, the child and human dominion |
| 9:1-20 | Psalm 7:8-17; 10:1-18; 96:10-13; 98:9; Acts 17:31; Revelation 19:11 | Parallel: thanks to the judge of the world |
| 10:1-18 | Psalm 9:9-10, 12-18; 22:1; 94:3-7; Romans 3:14; Job 21:7-15; Jeremiah 12:1-2 | Parallel: the hidden God and the wicked who prosper |
| 11:1-7 | Psalm 18:2; 27:1-6; 46:1-3; Genesis 19:24; Ezekiel 38:22; Habakkuk 2:20 | Parallel: refuge when the foundations are shaken |
| 12:1-8 | Psalm 5:9; 10:7; 18:30; 119:140; Proverbs 30:5; Isaiah 59:14-15; Micah 7:2 | Parallel: lies and the pure words of the LORD |
| 13:1-6 | Psalm 6:3; 35:17; 74:10; 79:5; 89:46; 90:13; Habakkuk 1:2; Revelation 6:10 | Parallel: how long, and the turn to trust |
| 14:1-7 | Psalm 53:1-6; Romans 3:10-12; Genesis 6:5, 12; 11:5; Psalm 126:1-6 | Parallel: the fool and the restoration from Zion |
| 15:1-5 | Psalm 24:3-6; Isaiah 33:14-16; Micah 6:6-8; Exodus 22:25; Ezekiel 18:5-9 | Parallel: who may enter the LORD's tent |
| 16:1-6 | Numbers 18:20; Deuteronomy 10:9; Psalm 73:25-26; 119:57; Lamentations 3:24 | Parallel: the LORD as portion and cup |
| 16:7-11 | Acts 2:25-31; 13:35-37; Psalm 17:15; 73:23-24; 49:15; Proverbs 15:24 | Parallel and allusion: the path of life and not seeing the pit |
| 17:1-15 | Deuteronomy 32:10-11; Psalm 7:1-5; 11:7; 16:11; 36:7-9; 63:1-8; Numbers 12:8 | Parallel: the prayer of the innocent and the sight of God's face |
| 18:1-50 | 2 Samuel 22:1-51; Exodus 15:1-18; Judges 5:4-5; Psalm 144:1-2; Romans 15:9; Hebrews 2:13 | Parallel: the royal thanksgiving, nearly the same as 2 Samuel 22 |
| 18:7-15 | Exodus 19:16-19; Judges 5:4-5; Psalm 29; 77:16-19; 97:2-5; Habakkuk 3:3-15 | Parallel: the appearing of God in the storm |
| 19:1-6 | Genesis 1:14-18; Psalm 8:1-3; 97:6; 104:1-4; Romans 1:19-20; 10:18 | Parallel: the heavens declare God's glory |
| 19:7-14 | Psalm 1:1-3; 119:97-104, 127; Deuteronomy 4:6-8; Proverbs 8:10-11; 6:23; Romans 7:12 | Parallel: the teaching of the LORD and the prayer for pardon |
| 20:1-9 | Psalm 33:16-19; 44:5-7; Isaiah 31:1-3; 2 Chronicles 20:1-12; 1 Samuel 17:45 | Parallel: the prayer for the king before battle |
| 21:1-13 | Psalm 20:1-9; 61:6-7; 72:1-17; 110:1-7; 1 Kings 3:11-14 | Parallel: the king's thanksgiving |
| 22:1-11 | Matthew 27:46; Mark 15:34; Psalm 13:1-2; 42:9-10; 71:5-6; Job 25:6; Isaiah 41:14; Lamentations 3:55-57 | Parallel and allusion: the cry of abandonment and the past trust |
| 22:7-8 | Matthew 27:39-43; Mark 15:29-32; Luke 23:35; Psalm 109:25; Lamentations 2:15 | Allusion: the mockers |
| 22:12-21 | Psalm 17:12; 35:15-17; 69:20-21; Matthew 27:35; John 19:23-24, 28; Hebrews 5:7 | Parallel and allusion: the bulls, the dogs and the garments |
| 22:22-31 | Hebrews 2:12; Psalm 66:13-20; 86:9; Isaiah 49:6; Revelation 7:9-10; Zechariah 14:9 | Parallel and allusion: praise among the congregation and the nations |
| 23:1-6 | Genesis 48:15; 49:24; Psalm 28:9; 80:1; 100:3; Isaiah 40:11; Ezekiel 34:11-16; John 10:11-16 | Parallel and allusion: the LORD as shepherd and host |
| 24:1-6 | 1 Corinthians 10:26; Psalm 15:1-5; 50:12; Isaiah 33:14-16; Matthew 5:8; Genesis 1:9-10 | Parallel: the earth is the LORD's and who may enter his place |
| 24:7-10 | 2 Samuel 6:12-15; Psalm 47:5-9; 68:24-27; 132:8; 118:19-20; 1 Chronicles 15:25-28 | Parallel: the entry of the King of glory |
| 25:1-22 | Psalm 34:1-22; 37:1-40; 86:1-17; 143:8-10; Exodus 34:6-7; Proverbs 3:32 | Parallel: the acrostic prayer for guidance and pardon |
| 26:1-12 | Psalm 7:3-5; 17:1-5; 24:3-4; 73:13; Deuteronomy 21:6; Job 31:1-40 | Parallel: the prayer of integrity |
| 27:1-6 | Psalm 23:4-6; 46:1-3; 84:1-4, 10; Isaiah 60:19-20; Micah 7:8; John 8:12 | Parallel: the LORD as light and the wish to dwell in his house |
| 27:7-14 | Psalm 13:1-3; 25:2-5; 31:22-24; 40:1-3; Isaiah 49:15; Lamentations 3:25-26 | Parallel: seeking the LORD's face and waiting |
| 28:1-9 | Psalm 12:2; 31:22; 55:21; 62:4; Jeremiah 9:8; Isaiah 40:11; Psalm 80:1 | Parallel: the prayer against deceit and the answer in praise |
| 29:1-11 | Psalm 18:7-15; 93:1-4; 96:7-9; 77:16-19; Exodus 19:16-19; Genesis 6:17 | Parallel: the voice of the LORD in the storm |
| 30:1-12 | Psalm 6:1-10; 9:13; 88:10-12; Isaiah 38:10-20; Isaiah 61:3; Jonah 2:1-10; Lamentations 3:31-33 | Parallel: thanksgiving for healing |
| 31:1-8 | Psalm 71:1-3; 25:1-3; 18:1-2; 23:3; Luke 23:46; Acts 7:59 | Parallel and allusion: refuge, and the commending of the spirit |
| 31:9-18 | Psalm 6:6-7; 38:11; 88:8, 18; Jeremiah 20:10; Lamentations 1:20; Psalm 69:19-20 | Parallel: the lament and the plots |
| 31:19-24 | Psalm 27:5, 13-14; 34:22; 36:7-9; Isaiah 64:4; 1 Corinthians 2:9 | Parallel and allusion: the goodness stored up and the call to be strong |
| 32:1-11 | Romans 4:6-8; Psalm 51:1-12; 38:1-22; Proverbs 28:13; 1 John 1:8-9; Psalm 130:3-4 | Parallel and allusion: the happiness of the forgiven |
| 33:1-22 | Genesis 1:3-9; Hebrews 11:3; Psalm 20:7; 32:11; 96:1-3; 147:10-11; Isaiah 40:12-17 | Parallel: the word of the LORD in creation, and trust |
| 34:1-10 | 1 Samuel 21:10-15; Psalm 25:1-3; 1 Peter 2:3; Psalm 23:1; Luke 6:20 | Parallel and allusion: David before Abimelech and the taste of the LORD's goodness |
| 34:11-22 | 1 Peter 3:10-12; John 19:36; Exodus 12:46; Psalm 37:27; 51:17; Isaiah 57:15 | Parallel and allusion: the fear of the LORD, and the broken-hearted |
| 35:1-10 | Exodus 14:14; Psalm 7:12-16; 9:15; 57:6; 70:2-3; 109:1-20 | Parallel: the LORD as advocate and warrior |
| 35:11-18 | Psalm 38:20; 109:4-5; Job 30:9-10; Matthew 26:59-60; Psalm 22:12-13, 21 | Parallel: the false witnesses and the repaid kindness |
